## 6.2 From the Publisher
Ritual discipline was and remains an important factor in the liturgical unity of every Church sui iuris (of its own right), one of which is the Ukrainian Catholic Church (UCC).
The Rite of the UCC was formed over centuries from the ancient Kyivan rite and today constitutes a rite distinct from others of Byzantine origin, which is often defined by the term "Byzantine-Ukrainian."
Ritual unity is achieved through the observance of unified rubrical prescriptions. From ancient times, the Eastern Churches began to use collections of such prescriptions, that is, Typikons (from the Greek word typikon).
In a Typikon, the most diverse combinations of services that may arise during the liturgical year are systematized, and precise instructions are given on how, in one case or another, to conduct the divine service "according to the rule" (ustavno), that is, correctly and in an exemplary manner.
In Ukraine, from the times of St. Theodosius of the Caves (†1074), the Studite Typikon (named after the Constantinopolitan monastery of St. Theodore the Studite) was in use, and from the 15th century, it was replaced by the Jerusalem Typikon (of the Lavra of St. Savva the Sanctified). In later centuries, domestic Typikons appeared: the Pochaiv Typikon of 1766, the Peremyshl Typikon of J. Doskovsky (editions of 1852, 1870, 1903), and the Uzhhorod Typikon (1890, 1901). Anonymous handwritten Typikons were also used.
In 1899, the world saw the Typikon of Fr. Isydor Dolnytsky (1830-1924), spiritual director and professor of the Greek-Catholic Theological Seminary in Lviv, author of many new services of the UCC. Compiled based on the materials of the Lviv Synod of 1891, which had immense significance in the history of our Church, Fr. Dolnytsky's Typikon presents rich practical and historical liturgical material.
It is generally known that the UCC began to be reborn on its historical lands only in 1989, after nearly 40 years of existence in the catacombs. This does not mean that after the "Lviv Sobor" of 1946, liturgical life of the Catholic Church in Ukraine ceased, but the long absence of communication, of contacts with the outside world, and with other parts of the UCC, took its toll in many areas of Church life, particularly in ritual matters.
In recent years, the decline of ritual discipline has been felt especially keenly. It was felt most in the Roman educational institutions of the UCC, to which students from Ukrainian eparchies all over the world come for studies.
The long-time rector of one such institution, the Ukrainian Papal College of St. Josaphat, Fr. Dr. Sofron Mudry, OSBM, inspired by the danger of losing ritual identity, initiated the realization of a new edition of Fr. Dolnytsky's Typikon, as the previous edition had long been out of print, yet the Typikon itself had not lost its practical significance. The language of the original, Church Slavonic, is already little understood today, so there was a palpable need for a translation of the Typikon into the modern Ukrainian language.
At the initiative of Fr. Dr. S. Mudry in 1979, the then spiritual director of the College of St. Josaphat, Fr. Rodion Holovatsky, OSBM, began purposeful work on the translation of the Typikon, which was interrupted by the sudden death of the Spiritual Father in July 1985. The translation, which was carried out with strict adherence to the original text, without any changes, was completed in its editing phase in 1992. Participating in the work on the translation were Fr. Dr. Parteniy Pavlyk (translation of Greek texts), Fr. Dorotey Shymchiy (notes), and Fr. Josaphat Romanyk (Part 2).
The final editing of the translation was reviewed by a commission composed of: Fr. Dr. Severian Yakymyshyn, consultor of the Liturgical Commission at the Congregation for the Eastern Churches; Fr. Dr. Porfiriy Pidruchny; and Fr. Mgr. Mykhailo Havryliv. By the decision of the commission, the "Rule of Divine Services" (Ustav bohosluzhen'), approved by the Apostolic See in 1944 and first published in the Ukrainian language in 1958, which replaced the first part of the Typikon, i. e., "General View of Divine Services," is added to the translation of the Typikon. However, this first part does not lose its significance as a valuable historical source of the development of the rite. We also note in which points the Typikon of Fr. Dolnytsky has lost its relevance today:
##### 1.	Liturgical prescriptions of Part 1 of Fr. Dolnytsky's Typikon, taken from the "Appendix" of the Lviv Synod published in 1896, are valid today only insofar as they agree with the Roman "Rule of Divine Services", or explain or supplement it in some way.
##### 2.	Rubrics concerning various fasts, given by Fr. Dolnytsky's Typikon, are no longer valid today, because in 1966 the Episcopate of the UCC, under the leadership of Major Archbishop and Cardinal Josyf Slipyj, issued new norms in which they obliged all clergy, monastics, and faithful in conscience to observe the fast, namely:
a) On all Fridays of the entire year, with the exception of compact weeks (zahalnytsia) and occurring Feasts of the Lord and the Theotokos - abstinence from meat;
b) On the first day of the Great Fast and on Good Friday - abstinence from meat and dairy foods;
c) On the Eve of the Nativity and Theophany, the Feast of the Exaltation of the Precious Cross, and the Beheading of the Precious Head of St. John the Baptist - abstinence from meat with a single meal to satiety with additional modest snacks, according to local customs;
His Beatitude the Major Archbishop permitted bishops, pastors, and confessors to grant other alleviations in case of need, according to the spirit of the rite (Herald of the Major Archbishop, 1966, vol. 2, Art. 195).
##### 3.	The Holy Liturgy is permitted to be abbreviated as presented in the Liturgicon (Sluzhebnyk) issued by the Synod of Bishops of the UCC in 1989, and the Apostle and Gospel are permitted to be taken as one [per reading], even if two fall on that day (Herald of the Major Archbishop, 1965, vol. 1, p. 42). With the blessing of the Rector (pastor), for the purpose of shortening the divine services, it is permitted to omit secondary Troparia and Kontakia if several fall on a certain day; with the same blessing, it is permitted not to take the same Troparion, Sessional Hymn, or the same Stichera twice or thrice.
The need for rubrical literature is constantly growing. The reason for this is the rebirth of the UCC on its historical lands; therefore, the editors believe and hope that the new edition of Fr. Dolnytsky's Typikon, now in the Ukrainian language, will serve for the best possible education of the new generation of clergy of our Church.
Like every work of such magnitude, this translation is not devoid of errors and flaws. The editors are grateful in advance to all those who send their remarks.
Publisher "Ukrainian Spiritual Library"
COMPONENT PARTS OF THE TYPIKON
This Typikon consists of five parts, of which Part 1 presents the general view of the divine service; Part 2 - general rubrics for various services of the Octoechos and Menaion; Part 3 - specific rubrics for certain services of the Menaion; Part 4 - specific rubrics for the services of the Triodion; Part 5 - specific rubrics for temple feasts.

--- Match 4 [Terms: local custom] ---
### 1.2.3 Order of Daily Vespers
#### 1.2.3.1 Rubrics during the entire Vespers
*Let it be known that at this Vespers there is no Entrance, therefore the Holy Doors are not opened, nor do deacons serve, nor does the Priest use the phelonion, but only the epitrachelion; he censes only at 'Lord, I have cried.' The Priest comes out to the front of the Holy Doors in the following cases:*
1. *At the beginning as at Great Vespers.*
2. *At all litanies (except the one Small Litany, which, according to local custom, he says quietly at his place).*
3. *At 'Wisdom! Upright!' and remains there until the end of the exclamation:*
   > **Priest**: "Let us be attentive! Peace be unto all!"

--- Match 5 [Terms: parish, in parish churches] ---
*...and the Choir immediately: 'More honorable' and everything else as at Great Vespers without Vigil. At 'Christ our True God' the Priest turns to the people.*
#### 1.2.3.3 Notes
1. *On Sunday itself or on another Feast with Vigil in parish churches in the province, the general custom remains to make an Entrance.*
2. *It is not proper to sit at this Vespers either, when it is not permitted to sit at Great Vespers without Vigil.*
________________________________________
### 1.2.4 Order of Small Vespers
*This Vespers is celebrated just as Daily [Vespers][^18], however, without a Kathisma and without litanies, except for the last 'Have mercy on us, O God,' which only the Slavonic Typikons present, but the Greek do not prescribe. The stichera at 'Lord, I have cried' will be only 4. The dismissal, although it can begin from 'Glory to Thee, O Christ God,' yet with us is exclaimed also before that:*
> **Priest**: "Wisdom!"

--- Match 6 [Terms: monks, in monasteries] ---
## 1.3 Compline
### 1.3.1 Order of Small Compline
#### 1.3.1.1 Text and Rubrics Flow
1. *The beginning is usual, that is:*
   > **Priest**: "Blessed is our God," etc.
   
   *...'Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee' and all other introductory prayers up to 'Come, let us worship' (3) inclusive, exactly as we presented at Great Vespers without Vigil*[^19].
2. *The Three Psalms, Small Doxology and Symbol of Faith just as in the Horologion.*
3. *The Canon to the Theotokos of the Octoechos, according to the tone and day of the week, without Katavasias and without litanies, has only after the 6th Ode 'Lord, have mercy' (3), Glory, Both now: Sessional Hymn, and sometimes also a Kontakion. [For personal use in the Prayer Book one Canon to the Theotokos of the Octoechos is given, of Tuesday Tone 8, and in it, after the 3rd Ode -- a Sessional Hymn, and after the 6th -- a Kontakion and Ikos].*
4. *After the Canon -- 'It is truly meet,' Trisagion and everything else with 'Our Father' and 'For Thine is the kingdom'.*
5. *Troparia for various occasions, that is:*
   * **a)** *On the first five weekdays, beginning with Monday;*
   * **b)** *on Saturday;*
   * **c)** *on Sunday;*
   * **d)** *for occasions of Feasts and a Polyeleos Saint.*
*On the first five days of the week the following six troparia are taken, that is first of the Temple, if it is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, after this the troparion of the weekday; if the Temple is not of the Lord and not of the Theotokos, but of a Saint, then first the troparion of the day, and after it of the Temple; and the other four troparia which are given in the Horologion, beginning from 'O God of our Fathers' with the subsequent three; monks before 'O God of our Fathers' recite also the troparion to St. Basil. On Thursday, because the service is double and has two troparia, there will be seven troparia, and in monasteries -- eight, with the troparion of St. Basil[^20]. On Wednesday and Friday, because the troparion is of the Lord, that is 'O Lord, save Thy people,' it in any case takes the first place, after it -- the troparion of the Temple.*
*On Saturday, that is on Friday evening for Saturday, the troparia are three: first of the day, and then Glory: Kontakion to the Dead, Both now: Kontakion of the day. Because on Saturday the temple service has greater significance than on other days of the week, which follows from the fact that it has a Canon at Matins, therefore for the sake of this the troparion of the day is preceded by the troparion of the Temple, if it is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, but not in the case where the temple is of a Saint, because His memory is already mentioned in the troparion of the day which is dedicated to all Saints.*
*On Sunday, that is from Saturday evening for Sunday, our Horologia present only the Hypakoe of the tone of the Sunday.*
*In the case of a festal service (Forefeast and Afterfeast of the Lord or of the Theotokos), and also in the case of a Polyeleos Saint, only the Kontakion of the named service is said.*
1. *After the troparia -- 'Lord, have mercy' (40) and everything else, as given in the Horologion.*
2. *At the end -- the dismissal, and after it -- the litany, as in the Horologion at the end of Daily Midnight Office (the petitions given before the litany are recited only in monasteries). Instead of this litany, we say today in private 'Remit, pardon'.*
#### 1.3.1.2 Rubrics
*This Compline, if it is celebrated in church, is celebrated only in the epitrachelion, with closed Holy Doors. The Priest comes out to the front of the Holy Doors at the beginning and remains there until 'Come, let us worship,' and again comes out at 'Thou Who at all times' and remains there until the end.*
*At the dismissal of this Compline, as at the dismissal of every other Divine Service, if it is celebrated in church, it is proper for the Priest to turn to the people.*
### 1.3.2 Order of Great Compline without Vigil
*The Order of this Compline is given in the Horologion or in the Prayer Book. And the rubrics for the Priest at it are such:*
* **a)** *He uses only the epitrachelion, first having blessed and having kissed the cross upon it.*
* **b)** *He stands before the Holy Doors: from the usual beginning to 'Come, let us worship' (3) inclusive; at all prayers, that is at the prayer of St. Basil 'O Lord, Lord'; at the prayer of Manasses 'O Lord Almighty,' at [the prayer] of Basil 'O Master God'; and from 'Thou Who at all times' to the end of Compline, inclusive of the Litanies. At the verses of the Song of Isaiah 'God is with us' and at the moleben verses 'O All-Holy Lady' He does not have to come out to the front of the Holy Doors, nor recite them alternately with the Choir, if there are two choirs, but only when there is only one choir.*
* **c)** *At the prayer 'O Master, Plenteous in Mercy' (instead of the dismissal) He does not turn to the people.*
* **d)** *This Compline, just as the Small, is celebrated with closed Holy Doors.*

--- Match 7 [Terms: local custom] ---
*...and, when the choirs sing 'God is the Lord' according to the tone of the troparion of that day, He sings the verses at 'God is the Lord'; then, having bowed, He withdraws through the southern door to His place in the sanctuary. After this, the troparia are sung in the following manner:*
#### 1.5.1.2 Troparia at "God is the Lord"
* **If it is a Feast of the Lord or of the Theotokos**: *then the troparion of the Feast twice, and also Glory, Both now: once more the same one.*
* **If the service is Sunday and of a Saint with his own troparion**: *then the Sunday troparion of the current tone twice, Glory: troparion of the Saint once, and Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday ones, according to the tone of the troparion of the Saint.*
* **If the service is Sunday and of two Saints with their own troparia**: *then the Sunday troparion of the current tone once, troparion of the first Saint once, Glory: troparion of the second Saint once, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday ones, according to the tone of the Doxastikon, that is of the second Saint*[^24]*.*
* **If the service is Sunday and of a Feast, and of a Saint**: *then the troparion of the current tone twice, Glory: troparion of the Saint once, Both now: troparion of the service of the Feast once*[^25]*.*
* **If the service is of a Saint on a weekday**: *then the troparion of the Saint twice, and also Glory, Both now: Theotokion, according to the tone of the troparion of the Saint, or from the Sunday ones if the Saint is a Polyeleos one, or a Dismissal one if [he is] non-Polyeleos, but then the Theotokion will be according to the tone of the troparion of the Saint and according to the day of the week.*
* **If two non-Polyeleos Saints**: *then the troparion of the first Saint twice, Glory: of the second once, Both now: Dismissal Theotokion, according to the tone of the second Saint and according to the day of the week.*
* **If on a weekday the service is of a Feast and of a Saint**: *then the troparion of the service of the Feast twice, Glory: troparion of the Saint once, Both now: once more the troparion of the Feast*[^3a]*.*
* **If on a weekday the service is of a Feast and of two Saints with their own troparia**: *then the troparion of the service of the Feast once, troparion of the first Saint once, Glory: of the second Saint once, and also Both now: troparion of the service of the Feast once*[^26]*.*
#### 1.5.1.3 From the Troparia to the Gradual
*After the troparia the Reader reads the current Kathisma, given according to the content in the Psalter (according to the current local custom, only a few verses). Then follows the Small Litany with the exclamation:*
> **Priest**: "For Thine is the dominion..."

--- Match 8 [Terms: local custom] ---
*...which according to local custom the Priest says quietly, sitting at his place, while the Choir sings its responses aloud; then follows the Sessional Hymn and Glory, Both now: its Theotokion.*

--- Match 9 [Terms: according to our custom] ---
*When the Gradual reaches the end, the Priest, having risen, vests in the phelonion, having blessed it before that and having kissed it where it has a cross (if he celebrates Matins separately from Vespers; if together, then it is sufficient for the Priest to bless and kiss the phelonion when he vests in it the first time, according to our custom, at the Entrance of Vespers), goes to the front of the steps of the Holy Table and, having bowed low to the Holy Mysteries, turns right to the Holy Doors, opens them and, having turned likewise by a half-turn to the Holy Table, exclaims, upon the conclusion of the Gradual:*
> **Priest**: "Let us be attentive! Peace be unto all!"

--- Match 10 [Terms: according to our custom] ---
##### 1.5.3.1 From the Beginning to the Canon
*From the beginning of Matins to the second Sessional Hymn inclusive everything happens just as at Great Matins without Vigil, with the exception of the censing at the beginning, which is prescribed in the Typikon, however, according to our custom of many years, is not practiced*[^40]*.* *After the second (or even the third)*[^41]* Sessional Hymn the 50th Psalm is taken and immediately the Canon just as given at Great Matins, except for the Katavasia, which will not be the current one, nor after every ode, but only after the 3rd, 6th, 8th and 9th - the heirmos of the last canon. At its 9th Ode, that is at 'My soul magnifies,' the Priest censes just as at Great Matins. After the Canon - 'It is truly meet'; after the Exaposteilarion - the Psalms of the Praises, simply, without singing and without the addition to the first two verses of the words: 'Let everything that hath breath' and 'To Thee belongs'; after Glory, Both now usually the Small Doxology is read, before which the Priest, having exited to the front of the Holy Doors says in the Forty Days: "To Thee belongs glory" and exclaims:*
> **Priest**: "Glory to Thee Who Hast shown us the light."

--- Match 11 [Terms: according to our custom] ---
*Regarding the commemoration which takes place at the dismissal of the Hours, let it be known that it is proper to commemorate the current services and as many as there are. For when two, or even three services coincide, it is proper to commemorate them all, one after another. If, for example, the Sunday service coincides with the Afterfeast of the Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos and of some Saint, then it is necessary to commemorate all, exclaiming:*
> **Priest**: "May Christ our True God, risen from the dead, through the prayers of His Most Pure Mother, whose Dormition we celebrate, and of the holy martyr (Name), whose memory we today honour..."
Here ends the text of the Lviv Synod.

## 1.7 On Vespers with Liturgy
*At Vespers which is sung with the Liturgy there will be an Entrance and, after the Prokimenon - readings; and then there will be neither a complete Vespers, nor a complete Liturgy. From Vespers its last part falls away, that is from the litany 'Let us say all' to its end; from the Liturgy, in turn, its initial part falls away, that is from the beginning to the Trisagion. Thus Vespers merges with the Liturgy as if into one order of Divine Service, and the connecting link of Vespers with the Liturgy at a Full Liturgy will be the Small Litany with the exclamation of the Trisagion, from which the Liturgy begins; and at the Liturgy of the Presanctified [Gifts] this link will be 'Let my prayer act,' which, according to our custom, the Priest sings at the four sides of the Holy Table; immediately after it follows the litany 'Let us say all,' before it, in the case of a Polyeleos Saint, will be his Apostle and Gospel, and on the first three days of Passion Week - the Gospel of the day.*

--- Match 12 [Terms: monasteries, in monasteries] ---
# PART II
GENERAL RUBRICS FOR VARIOUS SERVICES OF THE OCTOECHOS AND MENAION
There are 20 such rubrics, or paradigms of general divine services -- that is, 7 outside a feast, and 13 within a feast, as presented below:
OUTSIDE A FEAST
1.	Saint without Polyeleos on Sunday
2.	Saint without Polyeleos on weekdays, except Saturday
3.	Saint without Polyeleos on Saturday
##### 1.None.1 Saint with Polyeleos on Sunday
##### 1.None.2 Saint with Polyeleos on weekdays
##### 1.None.3 Saint with All-Night Vigil on Sunday
##### 1.None.4 Saint with All-Night Vigil on weekdays
WITHIN A FEAST
In a Forefeast
1.	Forefeast on Sunday with a Saint without Polyeleos
2.	Forefeast on a weekday with a Saint without Polyeleos
In a Feast
3.	Feast of the Lord on Sunday and weekdays
4.	Feast of the Theotokos on Sunday
5.	Feast of the Theotokos on weekdays
In an Afterfeast
6.	Afterfeast on Sunday with a Saint without Polyeleos
7.	Afterfeast on a weekday with a Saint without Polyeleos
8.	Afterfeast on Sunday with a Saint with Polyeleos
9.	Afterfeast on a weekday with a Saint with Polyeleos
10.	Afterfeast on Sunday with a Saint with All-Night Vigil[^44]
11.	Afterfeast on a weekday with a Saint with All-Night Vigil
In the Apodosis of a Feast
12.	Apodosis of a Feast of the Lord and the Theotokos on Sunday
13.	Apodosis of a Feast of the Lord and the Theotokos on a weekday
Note
A Saint without Polyeleos may be [sung] on 4 or on 6. The designation of a saint "on 4" or "on 6" depends on the number of troparia in each Ode of his Canon at Matins. There are three cases of a Saint without Polyeleos: that is, there may occur only one saint on 4, or two on 4, or one on 6. When we say one or two saints, we understand them as having separate services, for there may be several saints [commemorated], but they have only one service. The ecclesiastical rubric does not provide for more than two services to saints [on one day][^45].
Now we shall present the order for each of the 20 aforementioned cases.
## 2.1 Saint without Polyeleos on a Sunday
#### 2.1.1 AT Great Vespers
##### 2.1.1.1 Kathisma 1 *“Blessed is the man”* - whole, according to the Typikon. By our custom today, only certain selected verses of it are sung, which are contained in the church hymnal
##### 2.1.1.2 On *“Lord, I have cried”* - 10 stichera, that is, of the *Octoechos* of the resurrection of the current tone - 7 and of the *Menaion* (or *Anthologion*) to the saint, which is in the order of the *Menology* - 3. Also Glory, Both now: theotokion 1st of the current tone (Dogmatikon) from the *Octoechos*, or, as usual, from the *Heirmologion*, according to the musical notations. If there be a Doxastikon to the saint, then Glory: Doxastikon to the saint, also Both now: 1st theotokion of the current tone[^46]
  * **If two saints occur**, then there shall be 4 stichera of the *Octoechos* and of the *Menaion* to the first saint - 3, and to the second - 2, also Glory, Both now: 1st theotokion of the tone[^47]. If there be a Doxastikon to the first saint, then Glory: his Doxastikon, also Both now: 1st theotokion of the tone.
  * **If a saint on 6 occurs**, then there shall be 6 stichera of the *Octoechos* and of the *Menaion* to the saint - 4, also Glory: to the saint, and Both now: 1st theotokion of the current tone.
##### 2.1.1.3 Great Sunday Prokimenon *“The Lord is King”*[^48]
##### 2.1.1.4 On the Aposticha - stichera of the resurrection of the current tone. If there be a Doxastikon to the saint, then Glory: to the saint, Both now: theotokion of the Sunday Aposticha of the *Octoechos* in the tone of the Doxastikon[^49]
##### 2.1.1.5 Troparia: first of the resurrection of the current tone, also Glory: of the saint, Both now: theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the saint's troparion (New Greek Constantinopolitan Typikon, p. 77)
  * **If two saints occur**, then the troparion of the resurrection and of the first saint, Glory: of the second, Both now: theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the second[^50].
6.	the Great Dismissal with the commemoration of the Sunday service, of the first saint and of the second, if there be one[^51].
Note
For the convenience of finding the theotokion which is used at Vespers and at Matins in the tone of the saint's Doxastikon, at the end of every volume of the *Menaion* (as also in the *Anthologion*) are given all the theotokia that are taken on *“Lord, I have cried”* and on the Aposticha, and after the troparia of the saint, in the order of the eight tones and days of the week, primarily the Sunday ones, as well as the daily ones.
#### 2.1.2 AT COMPLINE
##### 2.1.2.1 Canon of the Theotokos of the *Octoechos* of the current tone, obligatorily as in the *Octoechos*, that is, every ode with the Heirmos on 5, without Katavasias and without Litanies, only after the 6th Ode - "Lord, have mercy" (3), also *“Glory, Both now”* and the Sessional Hymn (sometimes the Kontakion)[^52]
##### 2.1.2.2 Troparia after *“It is truly meet”* will not be said, unless one Hypakoe of the current tone from the *Horologion* (among the Sunday troparia)[^53]
##### 2.1.2.3 Dismissal: from *“Glory to Thee, O Christ God”* with the response *“Glory, Both now”*, "Lord, have mercy" (3), "Lord, bless"[^54]
AT THE Midnight Office
Canon of the *Octoechos* of the current tone, obligatorily as in the *Octoechos*, that is, with the Heirmos on 5, without Katavasias and without Litanies; only after the 3rd and 6th Ode - "Lord, have mercy" (3) and Sessional Hymn.
#### 2.1.3 AT Great Matins
##### 2.1.3.1 On *“God is the Lord”*: troparion of the resurrection of the current tone twice, Glory: of the saint, Both now: theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the saint's troparion
  * **If two saints occur**, then the troparion of the resurrection once and of the first saint once, Glory: of the second once, Both now: theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the second[^55].
##### 2.1.3.2 Kathismata: two appointed, after each of which - the Small Litany with the Sunday Sessional Hymns according to the *Octoechos*[^56]. Also Kathisma 17 ("The Blameless"), or 19 (Polyeleos): 17th - on Sundays between the Apodosis of the Holy Cross (September 21) and the Forefeast of the Nativity of Christ (December 20); 19th - on Sundays between the Apodosis of Theophany (January 14) and the 1st Sunday of Great Lent[^57]. After Kathisma 17 or 19 - immediately "The Angelic Host", Small Litany and Hypakoe of the current tone
##### 2.1.3.3 Gradual (Gradual) of the current tone from the *Octoechos*, or from the *Heirmologion
##### 2.1.3.4 Prokimenon of the resurrection of the current tone from the *Octoechos*, or from the *Heirmologion
##### 2.1.3.5 Gospel - one of the eleven Sunday Gospels
##### 2.1.3.6 Stichera of the resurrection "Jesus is risen"
##### 2.1.3.7 Canons 4 on 14: three of the *Octoechos* of the current tone on 10 and one of the *Menaion* to the saint on 4. Of the three canons of the *Octoechos*: the first, of the resurrection, is taken with the Heirmos on 4, the second, of the Cross-Resurrection - without the Heirmos on 3 and the third, of the Theotokos - also without the Heirmos on 3. At the end - the fourth from the *Menaion* of the saint, without the Heirmos on 4. For the Heirmoi of only the first canon are taken, and the others are not taken. They are given only to serve as a model for the singing of the troparia that follow them, which were once all sung according to their pattern. After every Ode - the designated Katavasia (Heirmos of the first canon of the feast[^58], for usually there are two canons), the scheme of which is given at the end of this Typikon. After the 3rd Ode - Kontakion and Ikos of the 6th Ode. Kontakion and Ikos of the saint, if there be (for the *Menaion* does not always give a Kontakion-Ikos), and Sessional Hymn of the saint. After the 6th Ode - Kontakion and Ikos of the resurrection, at the 8th Ode, before its Katavasia - the refrain "We praise", we sing to the melody of the refrain of the Idiomela hymns, in the tone of the Katavasia. At the 9th Ode we sing "My soul magnifies" with *“More honorable”*. After the 9th Ode - "Holy is the Lord our God" (3), in the tone of the current Prokimenon and the Exaposteilarion (Hymn of Light), one of the eleven, corresponding to the number of the Sunday Gospel, also Glory: Exaposteilarion of the saint, if there be, Both now: theotokion of the Sunday Exaposteilarion[^59]
  * **If two saints occur**, then there shall be two canons from the *Octoechos* on 6, that is: Resurrection with Heirmos on 4 and of the Theotokos on 2[^123], two of the *Menaion* on 8, that is, to the first saint on 4 and to the second on 4. After every Ode the designated Katavasia. After the 3rd Ode - Kontakion and Ikos of the first and second saint, if there be; also Sessional Hymn of the first without theotokion, Glory: of the second, Both now: his theotokion[^60]. After the 6th Ode - Kontakion and Ikos of the resurrection. After the 9th - Sunday Exaposteilarion, also, if there be in the *Menaion*, of the first saint, Glory: of the second, Both now: theotokion of the Sunday Exaposteilarion[^61].
  * **If a saint on 6 occurs**, then there shall be the Resurrection Canon with Heirmos on 4 and Cross-Resurrection on 2, of the Theotokos on 2 and of the saint on 6[^62].
##### 2.1.3.8 Praises (Lauds) stichera of the Sunday *Octoechos* of the current tone - 8, also Glory: Gospel sticheron, one of the eleven, corresponding to the Sunday Gospel of Matins, Both now: "Most Blessed Art Thou" from the *Horologion*, according to the rite of Matins. Refrains for the aforementioned 8 stichera are not all selected from the Psalms of Praise, but only 6, because for the 7th and 8th separate refrains are sung: for the 7th - "Arise, O Lord", and for the 8th - "I will confess"
  * **If the saint on 6 has** stichera on the Praises, then there shall be 4 of the resurrection and 4 of the saint with Glory, with two of his refrains[^63].
##### 2.1.3.9 After the Great Doxology - Sunday Troparion *“Today salvation has come to the world”*, when the tone is odd, that is 1st, 3rd, 6th, 9th, or *“Having risen from the tomb”*, when the tone is even, that is 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th
10.	the Great Dismissal, as at Vespers, with the commemoration of the Sunday service and of the saint, the first and the second, if there be.
#### 2.1.4 AT THE HOURS
##### 2.1.4.1 Troparia: Sunday - at all hours, also "Glory" at the 3rd and 9th - also to the saint, at the 6th - to the temple[^64] of the church. Kontakia: at the 1st and 9th - Sunday, at the 3rd - to the saint, at the 6th - to the temple
  * **If two saints occur**, then the Sunday troparion - at all hours, also "Glory" at the 3rd and 6th - to the saint, at the 6th - to the temple, at the 9th - to the second. Kontakia - alternating, that is: at the 1st - Sunday, at the 3rd - to the first, at the 6th - to the temple, at the 9th - to the second saint[^65].
##### 2.1.4.2 Dismissal: from *“Glory to Thee, O Christ God”*, with the commemoration of the Sunday and the saint, the first, and the second, if there be
#### 2.1.5 AT THE LITURGY
##### 2.1.5.1 Antiphons: the first two - Sunday, from the very day of the Resurrection, 3rd - the usual "Come", with the Sunday refrain (in monasteries - the Beatitudes)[^66]
##### 2.1.5.2 Sunday Troparion, Glory: Sunday Kontakion, Both now: Theotokion Kontakion, one of the feasts of the Theotokos, in the tone of the Doxastikon of the Sunday Kontakion, or the Kontakion of the church, if it is of the Theotokos, if - not, then the theotokion from the Sunday ones, in the tone of the Sunday Kontakion[^67], or the general one - "O Protection of Christians"[^68]
##### 2.1.5.3 Prokimenon, Alleluia - Sunday, of the current tone
##### 2.1.5.4 Apostle, Gospel - sequential Sunday
##### 2.1.5.5 Communion Hymn - Sunday "Praise the Lord"[^69]
Note
If a service for some need is also taken, it is preceded by the Sunday service. We replace the Kontakion of the supplication with the Theotokion of the supplication, if there is one, if - not, then with the Kontakion of the supplication, as the Lord's.
## 2.2 Saint without Polyeleos on Weekdays
AT VESPERS
##### 2.2.1 Kathisma current[^70]. On Sunday evening, in view of the All-Night Vigil which sometimes occurs, the Kathisma is not taken; on the eves of feasts for which the Typikon prescribes an All-Night Vigil, the current Kathisma is not taken
##### 2.2.2 On *“Lord, I have cried”* - 6 stichera[^130]: of the *Octoechos* of the current tone - 3[^71] and from the *Menaion* of the saint - 3. Glory, Both now: theotokion (or Stavrotheotokion) which follows the stichera of the saint. If in the *Menaion* there is a Doxastikon to the saint, then we sing Glory: Doxastikon, Both now: theotokion of the *Octoechos* in the tone of the Doxastikon and of the day of the week, from *“Lord, I have cried”*. Such a Doxastikon, according to the rubrics of the Greek Typikon, is sung by both choirs together ενοὐμένων των δυο χορών. (Καιφ. ε.)[^72]
  * **If two saints occur**, then the stichera of the *Octoechos* are not sung, but we sing from the *Menaion* to the first saint - 3 and to the second - 3, Glory, Both now: theotokion (or Stavrotheotokion) which is after the stichera of the second saint. If the first saint has a Doxastikon, then the theotokion is also selected for it, as above for a single saint.
  * **If a saint on 6 occurs**, then nothing is taken from the *Octoechos*, but we sing all 6 stichera only to the saint. To his Doxastikon we select the usual theotokion in the tone of the Doxastikon and of the day of the week.
##### 2.2.3 Prokimenon of the day of the week, which is in the order of Vespers in the *Horologion
##### 2.2.4 On the Aposticha: all stichera from the *Octoechos*, with the two refrains given in the *Horologion* in the order of Vespers; only Glory: to the saint, if there be in the *Menaion*, then Both now: theotokion of the Aposticha of the *Octoechos*, in the tone of the Doxastikon and the day of the week
##### 2.2.5 Troparion of the saint, Glory, Both now: Dismissal Theotokion from the *Horologion*, in the tone of the saint's troparion and of the day of the week[^112]
  * **If two saints occur**, then there will be the troparion to the first saint, Glory: to the second, Both now: Dismissal Theotokion in the tone of the second saint and of the day of the week[^73].
#### 2.2.1 AT COMPLINE
##### 2.2.1.1 Canon of the Theotokos of the *Octoechos*, of the current tone, exactly as in the *Octoechos*, that is, every ode with the Heirmos on 5, without Katavasias and without Litanies, only after the 6th Ode - "Lord, have mercy" (3), also Glory, Both now: Sessional Hymn
##### 2.2.1.2 Troparia after *“It is truly meet”*: of the temple, if it is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, and also of the day of the week, and the other four that are in the *Horologion* in the order of Small Compline. If the temple is of a saint, then first - of the day[^196], and then - of the temple. On Wednesday and Friday, if the temple is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, there is no need to say that of the temple first, because the Cross Troparion is of the Lord[^74]
AT THE Midnight Office: Everything exactly as in the *Horologion* for weekdays.
AT MATINS
##### 2.2.1.3 On *“God is the Lord”*: troparion of the saint twice, Glory, Both now: Dismissal Theotokion in the tone of the saint's troparion[^121][^119] and of the day of the week[^75]
  * **If two saints occur**, then the troparion to the first saint twice, Glory: to the second once, Both now: Dismissal Theotokion in the tone of the second saint[^76].
##### 2.2.1.4 Kathismata current, usually two, after each of which there is a small litany and a Sessional Hymn from the *Octoechos*[^77]
##### 2.2.1.5 Canons 3 on 14: first of the *Octoechos* with the Heirmos on 6, second of the *Octoechos* without the Heirmos, on 4, and of the saint of the *Menaion* without the Heirmos, on 4. For the Heirmoi are taken only of the first canon. Katavasia only after the 3rd, 6th, 8th and 9th Ode - Heirmos of the last canon, that is, of the saint[^78]. After the 3rd Ode - Sessional Hymn of the saint, after the 6th - Kontakion-Ikos of the saint, if he has one, after the 9th - Exaposteilarion from the *Octoechos*, also Glory: Exaposteilarion of the saint, if he has one, Both now: Theotokion of the Exaposteilarion of the saint. If the saint does not have an Exaposteilarion, then Glory, Both now: Theotokion of the Exaposteilarion of the *Octoechos*. Only on Wednesday and Friday, because the Exaposteilarion of the saint does not have a Stavrotheotokion, but only a Theotokion, the Stavrotheotokion of the Exaposteilarion of the *Octoechos* is taken. Also on Thursday, for then the Theotokion of the *Octoechos* is special, relating to St. Nicholas, whose service is leading on this day, the Theotokion of the *Octoechos* is taken, and not of the *Menaion*[^79]
  * **If two saints occur**, then there will be the first Canon of the *Octoechos* (for the second is not taken) with the Heirmos on 6; also the canon of the first saint, without the Heirmos, on 4, and of the second without the Heirmos, on 4[^80]. Katavasia after the 3rd, 6th, 8th and 9th Ode - Heirmos of the second canon[^81]. After the 3rd Ode - Kontakion-Ikos of the second saint, if he has one, then Sessional Hymn of the first saint without Theotokion, Glory: Sessional Hymn of the second saint, Both now: his Theotokion[^82]. After the 6th Ode - Kontakion-Ikos of the first saint, after the 9th Ode - Exaposteilarion of the *Octoechos*, also Exaposteilarion of the first saint, Glory: of the second, if he has one, Both now: his Theotokion[^83].
  * **If a saint on 6 occurs**, then there will be the first Canon of the *Octoechos* with the Heirmos on 4, not taking two Martyria in it (except Thursday, when there are no Martyria), also the second of the *Octoechos* on 4 and from the *Menaion* of the saint on 6[^84]. Everything else in the canon is as with one saint on 4.
##### 2.2.1.6 Aposticha - all *Octoechos*, only if there is a Doxastikon in the *Menaion* to the saint, then at "Both now" the Theotokion is taken from the Matins Aposticha in the tone of the Doxastikon and of the day of the week
##### 2.2.1.7 At the end: Troparion of the saint, Glory, Both now: Dismissal Theotokion in the tone of the saint's troparion and of the day of the week, different from the one that was at Vespers and at *“God is the Lord”
  * **If two saints occur**, then there will be the troparion of the first, Glory: of the second, Both now: Theotokion in the tone of the second and of the day of the week[^85].
Note about a Saint on 6
##### 2.2.1.8 Sometimes a saint on 6 has nothing more than a saint on 4, except for six troparia in the odes of the Canon of Matins and six stichera on *“Lord, I have cried”*, if it does not coincide with the service of some feast; for then there will be on *“Lord, I have cried”* 3 stichera to the feast and 3 to the saint (September 9)
##### 2.2.1.9 Sometimes he has also on the Aposticha of Matins, after the stichera of the *Octoechos*, one sticheron with its own refrain, and in addition to that a Doxastikon (November 17)
##### 2.2.1.10 Sometimes he has Praises stichera, numbering 4, and afterwards - the Small Doxology and the Aposticha of the *Octoechos*, in its own place (September 6 and October 7). However, in this case, the decision remains with the Rector: to take the Great Doxology without the Aposticha, or the Small with the Aposticha[^86]
##### 2.2.1.11 Sometimes he has not only Praises stichera and the Great Doxology, but also at *“Lord, I have cried”* the Dogmatikon in the tone of the Doxastikon and a whole proper Aposticha, and for the troparia - the Sunday Theotokion at Vespers and at Matins. At Matins - proper Sessional Hymns and at the Canon - the designated Katavasia after every Ode (October 23 and July 25). If there be a feast of the Lord or of the Theotokos on 6, then the Canon is only of this service, without the *Octoechos* (July 2 and August 16 and 31)
#### 2.2.2 AT THE HOURS
Troparia and Kontakia: at the 1st - of the day of the week, at the 3rd and 9th - of the saint, at the 6th - of the temple[^87].
  * **If two saints occur**, then the troparion and Kontakion at the 1st should be of the day of the week, at the 3rd - to the first saint, at the 6th - to the temple, at the 9th - to the second saint.
#### 2.2.3 AT THE LITURGY
According to our general custom everything is taken only of the day, that is:
##### 2.2.3.1 Troparion of the day of the week, also Glory: its Kontakion, Both now: Kontakion of the temple, if of the Lord or of the Theotokos. If the temple is of a saint - then the general Theotokion "O Protection" ("O Undisputed Intercessor")[^89], or one of the feasts of the Theotokos, of the tone[^90]. On Wednesday and Friday we do not select Theotokia, but replace the Troparion of the day with the Kontakion of the day with the refrain *“Glory, Both now”*, because this Kontakion is of the Lord
##### 2.2.3.2 Prokimenon, Alleluia, Communion Hymn - of the day (at the end of the Apostle or our *Sluzhebnik* (lit. "Service Book"))
##### 2.2.3.3 Apostle and Gospel - of the day of the week, sequential after the Descent of the Holy Spirit[^91]
Notes
##### 2.2.3.4 If the service is taken only to the saint, then the Theotokion or Kontakion of the temple is taken one of the aforementioned
##### 2.2.3.5 If the service is only for some specific need, then the Theotokion will be for the need, and where there is none, then the Troparion of the service for need is replaced by its Kontakion (with the refrain *“Glory, Both now”*); it does not have to be of the Lord
##### 2.2.3.6 If the service is taken both of the day and to the saint[^115], then the service of the day precedes the saint and there will be the troparion of the day, then - of the saint and Kontakion of the day, also Glory: Kontakion of the saint, Both now: Theotokion or Kontakion of the temple, one of the aforementioned. On Wednesday and Friday: the Kontakion of the saint is replaced by the Kontakion of the day
##### 2.2.3.7 If the service of the day or of the saint and for a need is taken, then the service of the day or of the saint precedes the service for need and there will be the troparion of the day or of the saint and for need, also Kontakion of the day or of the saint, Glory: Kontakion for need, Both now: Theotokion for need, if there be one. On Wednesday and Friday: the Kontakion for need is replaced by the Kontakion of the day; but when the need does not have a Theotokion, then there will be Glory: Kontakion of the day, Both now: Kontakion for need
##### 2.2.3.8 The service for need, according to the decree of the Lviv Synod[^92], must always be taken alone, except for the Apostle and Gospel of the day, which may be combined with the service for the dead and precede its Apostle and Gospel
##### 2.2.3.9 In cathedral churches, where the Divine Service is celebrated properly and daily, one should not leave out the Apostle and Gospel of the day and here it is better to read the Apostle-Gospel of the day, rather than of the saint, for the Apostle-Gospels of the saints are repeated more often during the year, but of the day - not[^93]
##### 2.2.3.10 If there be several Prokimena, Alleluias, Apostle-Gospels and Communion Hymns, then one must know how to take them. Know first, that in church typikons more than two are not given, with the exception of Apostle-Gospels, of which only in Slavonic typikons three are given, for the Slavs are richer in supplications than the Greeks. For where three readings coincide, the Greek typikons do not give the sequential one, but the Slavonic ones take it under the refrain of the first reading, as if one with it[^94]. When this is impossible, on the occasion of some great feast, then they order to take it on the day before under the refrain with the sequential one[^95]. However, this rule has been preserved among us perhaps only in cathedral churches, for in others usually only one Apostle-Gospel is taken, as also one service either of the day, or of the saint, or one for need. If, therefore, there be two Prokimena and two Alleluias, then both are taken under one heading, that is both Prokimena - before the refrain of the first reading, both Alleluias - after the completion of the second reading. However, the first Prokimenon is taken twice, namely: once - before and once - after the verse, the second - only once and without a verse, for in this case the Typikon does not give a verse. Similarly also "Alleluia": both of its verses are taken only of the first service, and of the second - only one first verse[^96]. The Heading, that is from whom: from Paul, from Matthew or from another, is taken only of the first reading, and the others usually are not taken, although it would be better to announce the headings of other readings as well, for it is not indifferent to the listeners to know the reading of which holy author is being proclaimed; and for this reason only would the rubric make certain sense, which orders to take the sequential reading under the refrain, for taking one reading under the refrain of another means merging it with the previous one because its heading is not announced. If the heading of the following third reading, which is not taken under the refrain, is not named, then it should have been said that this third reading is also taken under the refrain. "Let us be attentive" is announced only at the beginning of the first reading. If there be two Communion Hymns, then "Alleluia" is repeated only upon the completion of the second[^97]

--- Match 13 [Terms: according to our custom] ---
## 2.5 Saint with Polyeleos on Weekdays and Saturday
Note: The *Octoechos* is not used.
#### 2.5.1 AT Great Vespers
##### 2.5.1.1 Kathisma *“Blessed is the man”* (according to the Typikon - only the 1st antiphon)
##### 2.5.1.2 On *“Lord, I have cried”* - 6 stichera[^130] or 8[^131], all to the saint. Glory: to the saint, Both now: 1st Theotokion of the current tone, in view of its Apodosis. Prokimenon of the day from the *Horologion* and three readings to the saint
##### 2.5.1.3 Aposticha whole to the saint, with his refrains. To the Doxastikon of the saint the Theotokion is selected from the Sunday Aposticha in the tone of the Doxastikon[^132]
##### 2.5.1.4 At the end: Troparion of the saint, Glory, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the saint's troparion[^133]
5.	the Great Dismissal with the commemoration only of the service of the saint, but of the day - never, because in this case the service of the *Octoechos* is not taken.
#### 2.5.2 AT COMPLINE
Canon of the Theotokos from the *Octoechos* of the current tone[^134]. After *“It is truly meet”* with the Trisagion - Kontakion to the saint alone[^135].
#### 2.5.3 AT Great Matins
##### 2.5.3.1 On *“God is the Lord”*: Troparion to the saint twice, Glory, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday ones, in the tone of the saint's troparion
##### 2.5.3.2 Kathismata - two current with Sessional Hymns of the saint, also Kathisma 19, that is Polyeleos with Magnifications and the Post-Polyeleos Sessional Hymn of the saint
##### 2.5.3.3 Gradual of Tone 4, first antiphon
##### 2.5.3.4 Prokimenon, Gospel and Sticheron to the saint
  * The Typikon of the Pochaiv *Anthologion*, instead of "Through the prayers of the holy Apostles" gives: "Through the prayers of the saint (name), whose memory is celebrated (See the service of a prophet). So gives also the Greek-Venetian *Menaion* (See e. g. January 11, 25, 27, 30). However, general editions do not give this.
##### 2.5.3.5 Canons two: 1st - of the Theotokos, one of the two usual ones ("Having traversed the moisture", Tone 2 of Sunday Matins, or "Having crossed the water", Tone 8 of Compline on Tuesday) with the Heirmos on 6, then to the saint one or two canons on 8[^136]
  * On Saturday, if the temple is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, the canon of the temple is taken with the Heirmos on 6, Heirmos - twice, and to the saint on 8[^137].
Katavasia current after every Ode. After the 3rd Ode - Sessional Hymn of the saint, after the 6th - Kontakion-Ikos of the saint, after the 9th - Exaposteilarion of the saint.
##### 2.5.3.6 Praises stichera - 4 to the saint, Glory: his Doxastikon, Both now: Theotokion, which is after the Doxastikon of the saint
##### 2.5.3.7 After the Great Doxology with the Trisagion - Troparion to the saint, Glory, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the saint's troparion
8.	the Great Dismissal, as at Vespers.
AT THE HOURS: At all hours: Troparion and Kontakion of the saint.
AT THE LITURGY: Everything - only to the saint. Apostle and Gospel first - of the day, then - to the saint.
## 2.6 Saint with an All-Night Vigil on a Sunday
AT SMALL VESPERS
##### 2.6.1 On *“Lord, I have cried”*: stichera - 4, all Sunday of the current tone of the *Octoechos*; also Glory: sticheron to the saint, Both now: Theotokion of the tone of Small Vespers[^138] that is 1st Theotokion of the tone[^139], which is called Dogmatikon[^140]
##### 2.6.2 On the Aposticha stichera - 4, that is: one Sunday, which is in the first place in the *Octoechos* of the current tone and 3 to the saint with two of his refrains, which are on the Aposticha of Great Vespers[^141], also Glory: to the saint from his Small Vespers, Both now: Theotokion from the Aposticha of Small Vespers[^142]
##### 2.6.3 At the end: Sunday Troparion, Glory: to the saint, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the saint's troparion
##### 2.6.4 Dismissal with the commemoration of the Sunday and of the saint
#### 2.6.1 AT Great Vespers
##### 2.6.1.1 Kathisma 1 *“Blessed is the man”* (according to the Typikon - whole, according to our custom today - only certain selected verses)
##### 2.6.1.2 On *“Lord, I have cried”* stichera - 10, that is 4 from the *Octoechos* and 6 to the saint, Glory: to the saint, Both now: 1st Theotokion of the current tone
##### 2.6.1.3 Prokimenon of the day from the *Horologion* and 3 readings to the saint
##### 2.6.1.4 On the Lytia: according to the Slavonic Typikon, first sticheron - of the temple (which today we usually do not take), also stichera to the saint. Glory: to the saint, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday Aposticha of Great Vespers, in the tone of the Doxastikon[^153][^144][^143]
##### 2.6.1.5 On the Aposticha: stichera of the Sunday tone, Glory: to the saint, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday Aposticha of Great Vespers, in the tone of the Doxastikon
##### 2.6.1.6 At the Blessing of Loaves: Troparion "O Virgin Theotokos" twice and to the saint once[^227][^145]
#### 2.6.2 AT Great Matins
##### 2.6.2.1 On *“God is the Lord”*: Sunday Troparion twice, Glory: to the saint, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the saint's troparion
##### 2.6.2.2 Two current kathismata with their Sunday Sessional Hymns of the *Octoechos*; also Kathisma 19 (Polyeleos) with Magnifications and "The Angelic Host", and Hypakoe of the tone, and immediately all three Sessional Hymns of the saint, that is 1st and 2nd without Theotokion, then Glory: 2nd Sessional Hymn, which is called the Polyeleos one, Both now: its Theotokion[^147]
##### 2.6.2.3 Gradual, Prokimenon, Gospel and Sticheron, all only Resurrection Sunday
  * During the singing of the canon, by our custom, the Priest anoints the people with oil blessed at Vespers.
##### 2.6.2.4 Canons three or four on 14, namely: Sunday with Heirmos on 4 and of the Theotokos on 2, and to the saint on 8, there will be two or only one canon; for usually the saint has two canons[^148]
Katavasia current after every Ode. After the 3rd Ode - Kontakion-Ikos, and Sessional Hymn to the saint. After the 6th - Kontakion-Ikos Sunday, after the 9th - Sunday Exaposteilarion, Glory: to the saint, Both now: Theotokion of the Sunday Exaposteilarion
Everything else at Matins, at the Hours and at the Liturgy so, as with a Polyeleos saint, here on page 73.

--- Match 14 [Terms: which today we usually do not take] ---
## 2.7 Saint with an All-Night Vigil on Weekdays and Saturday
AT SMALL VESPERS
##### 2.7.1 On *“Lord, I have cried”* and on the Aposticha all stichera - to the saint, on the Aposticha there will be separate refrains to the saint. Only regarding the Theotokia on *“Lord, I have cried”* and on the Aposticha let it be known, that they sometimes will be special[^150], sometimes only general, designated only by the first words; sometimes even without indication of the first words. And in this last case, we think that they should be selected from the *Octoechos* on Daily Vespers in the tone of the Doxastikon and of the day of the week, which are given at the end of every volume of the *Menaion* and of the Lviv *Anthologion*; if - not, then in the tone of one of the three Doxastika, given at the end of the Pochaiv *Anthologion*[^151]
##### 2.7.2 At the end: Troparion of the saint, Glory, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday ones in the tone of the saint's troparion
#### 2.7.1 AT Great Vespers
1.	*“Blessed is the man”* (according to the Typikon - only the 1st antiphon).
##### 2.7.1.1 On *“Lord, I have cried”*: stichera of the saint on 8, Glory: His Doxastikon, Both now: 1st Theotokion (Dogmatikon), in the tone of the Doxastikon; on Friday for Saturday - Dogmatikon of the current tone[^152], in view of the Apodosis of the tone
##### 2.7.1.2 Prokimenon of the day from the *Horologion* and 3 readings to the saint
##### 2.7.1.3 On the Lytia: first sticheron - of the temple, which today we usually do not take, all the rest - to the saint and Glory: to the saint, Both now: Theotokion or special, if there be, or general from the Aposticha of Sunday Great Vespers, in the tone of the Doxastikon[^153]
##### 2.7.1.4 On the Aposticha all stichera - to the saint, with his two refrains; Theotokion to the Doxastikon, if there be no special one, is taken from the Sunday Aposticha in the tone of the Doxastikon[^154]
##### 2.7.1.5 At the Blessing of Loaves: Troparion of the saint twice and "O Virgin Theotokos" once
AT Great Matins, AT THE HOURS AND AT THE LITURGY
Everything so, as in the case of a Polyeleos saint without All-Night (see page 75-76); only Matins begins from "Glory to the Holy", and there will be anointing with oil during the canon, according to custom.
Note
Three feasts, namely: The Nativity and Beheading of St. John the Baptist on June 24 and August 29 and of the Chief Apostles Peter and Paul on June 29 surpass other saints with All-Night Vigil in that at Matins their canons are taken alone, without the Theotokion canon, and at the Liturgy their Apostle-Gospels are taken alone[^155], without the daily ones, which are read on the previous day under the refrain, except for Sundays, for these always precede. According to today's general custom, except for cathedral churches, on other saints too the daily Apostle-Gospels are not read. To these three feasts the Moscow Typikon adds also the feast of John the Theologian and the Feast of the temple[^156].
CASES OF FEASTS
## 2.8 Forefeast with a Saint without a Polyeleos on a Sunday
Note: If two saints with a Forefeast fall on a Sunday, then the service of the first (second) is transferred to another day, according to the decision of the Ecclesiarch[^157].
#### 2.8.1 AT Great Vespers
1.	*“Blessed is the man”* (according to the Typikon - all three antiphons).
##### 2.8.1.1 On *“Lord, I have cried”* - 10 stichera, that is: of the *Octoechos* of the current tone - 4, of the Forefeast - 3 and to the saint - 3. Glory: of the Forefeast, Both now: 1st Theotokion of the tone. If there be a Doxastikon to the saint, then having discarded the Doxastikon of the Forefeast, Glory: Doxastikon to the saint is taken, Both now: 1st Theotokion of the tone[^158]
  * **If the saint be on 6**, then there will be of the Sunday - 3, of the Forefeast - 3 and to the saint - 4, Glory: to the saint, Both now: 1st Theotokion of the tone[^159].
##### 2.8.1.2 On the Aposticha: stichera Sunday, Glory, Both now: of the Forefeast. If there be a Doxastikon to the saint, then after it at "Both now" is taken of the Forefeast
##### 2.8.1.3 At the end: Sunday Troparion, Glory: of the saint, Both now: of the Forefeast
5.	the Great Dismissal with the commemoration of the Sunday service and of the saint[^160].
AT COMPLINE: After *“It is truly meet”* - Kontakion of the Forefeast alone[^161].
AT THE Midnight Office: Trinitarian Canon of the current tone and everything else, as usual.
#### 2.8.2 AT Great Matins
##### 2.8.2.1 On *“God is the Lord”*: Sunday Troparion twice, Glory: to the saint, Both now: of the Forefeast
##### 2.8.2.2 Everything else up to the canon - Sunday, as usual
##### 2.8.2.3 Canons 4 on 14, that is: first - Sunday of the *Octoechos* with Heirmos on 4 and second - of the *Octoechos* of the Theotokos on 2, also of the Forefeast on 4 and of the saint on 4[^162]. (If the saint - on 6, then here is taken only on 4). Katavasia after every Ode - current. After the 3rd Ode - Kontakion-Ikos of the Forefeast and of the saint, Glory: Sessional Hymn of the saint, Both now: of the Forefeast. After the 6th Ode - Kontakion-Ikos Sunday. After the 9th - "Holy is the Lord our God" thrice and Sunday Exaposteilarion, Glory: of the saint, Both now: of the Forefeast[^163]
##### 2.8.2.4 Praises stichera - 8, that is: of the *Octoechos* - 4 and to the saint - 4, if he has, with two separate refrains of his; if the saint does not have, then of the feast on 4 (from the Aposticha with their refrains); also Glory: sticheron of the feast, Both now: "Most Blessed Art Thou"[^164]
##### 2.8.2.5 After the Doxology - Sunday Troparion, one of two usual ones
6.	the Great Dismissal, as at Vespers.
#### 2.8.3 AT THE HOURS
At all: Sunday Troparion, also Glory: at the 1st and 3rd - also of the Forefeast, at the 6th and 9th - also to the saint. Kontakion, Sunday and of the feast, we say alternatingly, that is at the 1st and at the 6th - of the Forefeast, and at the 3rd and 9th - Sunday[^165].
#### 2.8.4 AT THE LITURGY
All service - only of the Sunday, except Troparia, for they are taken also of the Forefeast with the Sunday ones, that is Sunday and of the Forefeast, Glory: Sunday Kontakion, Both now: of the Forefeast[^166].

--- Match 15 [Terms: According to our custom] ---
#### 3.1.4.4 Exaltation of the Precious Cross and Veneration of It
TRANSFER. When the stichera of the Praises are sung, the Priest with the Deacon or deacons, if there be [any], withdraw to the sacristy and vest in all sacred vestments of their rank of crimson color, according to the Lviv Synod, violet, exactly as for the Liturgy. At the end of the stichera they go before the steps of the altar and, having bowed low, open the Holy Doors. The Priest exclaims: "Glory to Thee, Who Hast shown us the light". The choirs sing the Great Doxology, and the Priest goes out through the Holy Doors to the tetrapod, takes the Gospel and carries it to the Holy Table. Having taken the censer, he censes the precious Cross crosswise three times, gives the censer to the Deacon and, having bowed low, takes the precious Cross with both hands and, having rested it on his head, basil to himself, Cross to the people, goes out before the steps and, preceded by two candle-bearers and the Deacon with the censer, goes around the altar from its right side, makes an entrance through the northern doors and stands before the Holy Doors between the deacons and behind the candle-bearers and there awaits the end of the Doxology and Trisagion. At their end the Deacon, and if there is none, then the Priest himself loudly exclaims: "Wisdom, attend!". Then they turn, go before the tetrapod by the right side: first the candle-bearers, behind them - the Deacon with the censer, and at the end - the Priest. The Priest places the precious Cross on a pillow at the top of the tetrapod, covered with a tablecloth or a costly covering, censes it from four sides, and the choirs sing the Troparion "Save, O Lord".
EXALTATION: After the singing of the Troparion and censing of the Precious Cross, the Priest takes the precious Cross with both hands and, standing before the tetrapod, facing east, sings  solemnly and joyfully the first petition of the litany "Have mercy on us, O God". Then, having signed with the precious Cross three times crosswise to the east, He begins to slowly bow His head to the ground, as much as possible (according to the rule - so that the head is a span away from the ground"). The Choir begins to sing in a quiet and humble voice, according to the rule, the first hundred, according to the present [custom], eight triplets, that is 24 "Lord, have mercy", lowering the voice with each triplet until it finishes the fourth triplet, then it raises the voice with each of the three following triplets, and the Priest gradually rises; when the Choir begins to sing the last triplet in the strongest voice and changed melody, the Priest, having again signed three times with the Cross to the east, moves to the south side of the tetrapod and, looking to the north, sings the second petition of the litany and performs an exaltation, similar to the first, also from the east side to the west and from the north to the south, and again, from the west to the east, singing at each petition of the litany, and performs an exaltation similar to the first. And Glory, Both now is sung: "Thou Who Wast lifted up" ("Voznesyisya").
VENERATION. When the Kontakion of the Cross is sung, the Priest places the Precious Cross on the tetrapod and, at the end of the Kontakion begins to sing "Before Thy Cross" three times. All Who sing bow with their forehead (the sacred minister only with His hand) to the ground after each singing of the troparion. When the Choir sings the stichera of the Cross, the Priest bows once, kisses the Precious Cross and, having bowed again, stands in his place, waiting until other sacred ministers also bow and kiss the Precious Cross. After this all together with the Priest bow and enter the sanctuary. Other sacred dignitaries, and after them the people, approach and kiss the Precious Cross one by one, bowing before and after this. At the end of the stichera of the Cross the Deacon, if there be one, if - not, then the Priest himself sings two usual litanies: "Have mercy on us, O God" and "Let us complete". Great Dismissal, as usual, with commemoration of the feast.
Note: According to our custom such veneration takes place after every service until the apodosis of the feast. However, the Priest performs the veneration in such vestments in which he happens to celebrate the service: sometimes in all priestly vestments, if he performs the veneration at the Liturgy, sometimes only in epitrachelion and phelonion, if he venerates, for example, at Great Vespers, sometimes only in epitrachelion, if he venerates, for example after Great Vespers without an entrance, or after the 1st Hour, except the 1st Hour of the day of the feast itself, for then we do not perform the veneration, since it already took place at Matins. The stichera of the Cross are sung during the Afterfeast only when there is a sufficient number of people present.

--- Match 16 [Terms: ancient rule] ---

If there be no deacons, then the Priest himself sings the litany
3.	"Blessed is the man" and the Small Litany, for which the second deacon goes out before the Holy Doors, and the Priest - the exclamation from his place. If there be no deacon, then the Priest himself, according to local custom, reads the litany quietly, sitting in his place, and the Choir sings its responses.

AT MATINS
Note: We begin prostrations from the Kathismata and make: 1. At "Alleluia" of each "Glory" of the Kathismata - 3 prostrations[^512]. 2. At "More honorable" of each of the 6 verses of the Song of the Theotokos - 1 prostration. 3. At "It is truly meet" - 1 prostration. 4. At "Glory to God in the highest" - 3 prostrations. 5. At the final Trisagion - 3 prostrations. 6. At the prayer of St. Ephrem - 16 prostrations, that is 12 small and 4 great.
RUBRIC
##### 4.1.7.1 From the beginning to the end of the litany "In peace let us pray to the Lord", everything, just as on every day
##### 4.1.7.2 Instead of "God is the Lord" we sing Alleluia in the sequential tone of the Octoechos
##### 4.1.7.3 We sing three Trinity Hymns of the current tone, given in the Horologion, three for each tone. They are all sung three times with the refrain "Glory" to the second, and "Both now" - to the third. At each first one we make a commemoration of the weekday service, at the second - a commemoration of all saints, at the third - a commemoration of the Theotokos. However, since the weekday service of the week changes daily, and the troparia are sung the same each day of the week (except Saturdays and Sundays, on which, because they are not Lenten, the Trinity Hymns are not sung), at the end of each first Trinity Hymn a colon is given, which signifies that the troparion must be completed with the commemoration of the weekday service, the form of which is indicated at the end of the 3rd troparion of Tone 1
Note
These three commemorations we make also at the Exaposteilaria following the canon. But the Lenten Exaposteilarion has only one troparion in each tone, which is sung three times, therefore, when it is sung the first time, the commemoration of the weekday service is added to it, when - the second time, with the refrain "Glory", then - the commemoration of all saints, and when the third time, with the refrain "Both now", then - the commemoration of the Theotokos. These commemorations are added where the colon is given, that is before "And save me".
##### 4.1.7.4 Three Kathismata with their Sessional Hymns, the Sessional Hymns of which, sung after the 1st Kathisma, are of the Octoechos of the current tone, given also at the end of the Triodion. The Sessional Hymns sung after the 2nd and 3rd Kathismata are of the Triodion
##### 4.1.7.5 Psalm 50 and the first petition of the Great Litany "Save, O God", and after the exclamation "Through the mercy and compassion" immediately - the Canon
##### 4.1.7.6 Odes and Canons. The Biblical Odes, given at the end of the Psalter, are intertwined with the odes of the canon, as given: before the ode of the canon the corresponding ode of the Psalter is recited, leaving its last verses for refrains to the troparia of the canon, in such quantity as there will be troparia in the ode of the canon, including the heirmos, except two, for to the penultimate troparion we refrain "Glory", and to the last - "Both now". The 2nd Ode is recited in full before the ode of the canon, and to the troparia of its canon we refrain: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee". According to our ancient custom, the Odes of the Psalter are recited only in those odes in which the triodion occurs, and in the other odes we refrain the usual refrains to the troparia of the canon. The Greek Typikon prescribes singing the Odes of the Psalter in the tone of the Menaion canon, but now we do not even know what their melody was
The Canons will be only of the Menaion and Triodion, for the Octoechos, according to the Greek rubric, during Great Lent is not sung, with the exception of Sundays. In some odes there will be 3 canons on 14, that is of the Saint of the Menaion with heirmos on 6 and both canons of the Triodion on 8: one on 4 and the second on 4. In some odes there will be only one canon of the saint on 4, and in the second ode there will be only two canons of the Triodion on 8, with heirmos on 9. This difference regarding the number of canons and their troparia in the odes arises because the Triodion canons do not occur daily in all odes, but in general only in three odes in the following sequence:
On Monday: 1st, 8th and 9th.
On Tuesday: 2nd, 8th and 9th.
On Wednesday: 3rd, 8th and 9th.
On Thursday: 4th, 8th and 9th.
On Friday: 5th, 8th and 9th.
On Saturday: 6th, 7th, 8th and 9th.
Therefore, there will be:
On Monday: at Odes 1, 8, and 9 - 3 canons on 14, that is of the Menaion with heirmos on 6 and both canons of the triodion on 8, and afterwards - one more separate troparion of the Triodion with the refrain "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee", and the Katavasia - Heirmos of the 2nd canon of the triodion. At other odes there will be only one canon of the saint on 4, with heirmos on 5; only at Odes 3 and 6 the canon of the saint is read without the heirmos, for it is taken afterwards, as Katavasia[^513].
On Tuesday: at the 2nd Ode, since the saint does not have this ode, there will be only two canons of the triodion on 8, and afterwards - one more separate troparion and Katavasia - Heirmos of the 2nd canon of the triodion; at the 8th and 9th Odes - 3 canons on 14 exactly as on Monday. At other odes there will be only the canon of the Menaion on 4 exactly as on Monday.
On Wednesday: at the 3rd, 8th, and 9th Odes there will be 3 canons on 14 exactly as at the 1st, 8th, and 9th Odes on Monday. And at other odes there will be only one canon of the saint on 4, as on Monday.
On Thursday: at the 4th, 8th, and 9th Odes there will be 3 canons on 14, as on Monday. At other odes - only the canon of the saint on 4 and the rest, as on Monday.
On Friday: at the 5th, 8th, and 9th Odes there will be 3 canons on 14, as on Monday; and at other odes - only one of the saint on 4.
On Saturday: at the 6th, 7th, 8th, and 9th Odes there will be 3 canons: one of the saint and two of the Triodion, at other odes there will be two canons: of the temple and of the saint; however, for Saturday a separate rubric will be given, since Saturday is not a Lenten day.
•	If two saints fall, then we read both their canons together on 6[^514], that is of the first saint with heirmos on 3 and of the second without heirmos on 3. So also to the saint on 6.
The Katavasia will be always the heirmos of the last canon either of the triodion, or of the Menaion: of the triodion where there will be a triodion, and where there will be only the Menaion, then the Katavasia of the Menaion will be only after Odes 3 and 6, for at the 8th and 9th there will always be a triodion and therefore there will always be the Katavasia of the triodion.
A more precise example of the combination of the Odes of the Psalter with the Odes of the Canon is given at the 1st Ode in the Moscow Typikon, and is presented as follows: "After this we begin: 'Let us sing to the Lord' in the tone of the canon of the Menaion of the saint of the day and the verses of the odes are recited hurriedly, each Choir - its verse, until they reach 'The waters became thick' and from this they begin verses on 14, and we sing the Menaion with heirmos on 6. And we sing thus: the first Choir reads the verse 'The waters became thick' and sings the heirmos. The second Choir reads the second verse 'The enemy said' and, after it, - the troparion of the canon, and in sequence verses by choirs up to 'Then hastened'. From the verse 'Then hastened' we begin the triodion of Kyr Joseph on 4. And at 'The Lord reigning' we sing the 2nd triodion of Kyr Theodore the Studite, and one choir sings the troparion, and the second choir - the 2nd verse and the troparion. After this both choirs, having united, sing: Glory: Trinity Hymn, in a higher voice, Both now: Theotokion. Then we sing in the highest voice: 'Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee', and we sing the other troparion of Theodore, because the ode has 5 troparia. At the end we sing the Katavasia - heirmos of the second canon"[^515].
Regarding the refrains to the troparia of the canon, know that in order to find the number of refrains as quickly as possible, the last four, six, and eight verses of the Biblical Odes (except the 2nd, for it is read in full before the heirmos) from the bottom up are marked in the middle of the song, or on the side, with the inscription: "on 4, on 6, on 8". But on 14 they are not counted off, since 6 verses from 8 upwards are easily counted. However, the counting of 14 verses in current Psalters does not coincide exactly with the counting in previous Psalters, for where the rubrics place the 14th verse at "The waters became thick", current Psalters place the 14th verse at "The enemy said", and "The waters became thick" moves to the 15th verse. The 6th Ode has only 12 verses, so do not forget to divide these two verses of it on 4. The 9th Ode, one, of the Theotokos, is sung whole before the heirmos with "More honorable", and the other, of Zachariah, is sung whole in the refrains, and in those editions where there are only 13 verses, one verse must be divided into 2.
The last edition of the Lviv Psalter does not give the counting of the last verses, except for the 9th Ode; and, in general, in recent editions they did not care about the counting of verses.
After the 3rd Ode - Sessional Hymn of the Saint[^548], after the 6th - Kontakion and Ikos of the Saint; if the saint does not have this, then the Martyric Sessional Hymn of the Octoechos of the current tone, given also at the end of the Triodion. After the 9th Ode - "It is truly meet" and after the litany - Exaposteilarion of the tone, which is in the Horologion, three times, with refrains and commemorations, as the Trinity Hymns, here on page 338, in the note.
##### 4.1.7.7 Before the Small Doxology the Priest exclaims: "To Thee belongs glory" and the rest, and at "Glory to God in the highest" we make three prostrations
##### 4.1.7.8 Aposticha of the Triodion, Glory: of the saint, if there be, Both now: Usual Theotokion in the tone of "Glory"
3.	"It is a good thing" twice; at the Trisagion - 3 prostrations and, after "Our Father", - Troparion "Standing in the temple"; then "Lord, have mercy" (40) and the rest with 16 prostrations at the prayer of St. Ephrem and, after the last prostration, immediately - "O come, let us worship" three times and the 1st Hour.

--- Match 20 [Terms: local custom] ---
### 4.1.8 Vespers on Wednesday and Friday with Presanctified
Note on Prostrations
On Wednesday we make 3 small prostrations: at the Trisagion at the beginning, at "O come, let us worship", at "Alleluia" after the pre-introductory psalm and after each antiphon of the Kathisma and at "Let my prayer be set forth". Great prostrations, according to local custom, we make at "Light of Christ", when the Priest blesses the people crosswise with the triple-candle; at the conclusion of the singing "Let my prayer be set forth" and after "Now the Powers of Heaven", when the consecrated Lamb is taken from the artophorion and placed on the diskos[^526]. And on Fridays at Vespers and at Compline we do not make prostrations, only the prescribed ones at the Presanctified, that is: after "Let my prayer be set forth" - 3 prostrations[^527], after the Entrance of the Holy Gifts - 3 prostrations and at "Let my prayer be set forth" - 3 prostrations, and among us also at "Light of Christ" - 3 prostrations.
RUBRIC
##### 4.1.8.1 After "Blessed is the Kingdom" we recite "O Heavenly King", Trisagion (with prostrations) and, after "Our Father", - "Lord, have mercy" (12), Glory, Both now: "O come, let us worship" three times (with prostrations)
##### 4.1.8.2 Of the eight prayers recited at the prefatory psalm, the Priest recites here only the last 5, for the first three he has to read afterwards, one at each of the three antiphons of the Kathisma
3.	18th Kathisma, with a prayer at each of its three antiphons and with a Small Litany after each antiphon.
##### 4.1.8.3 On "Lord, I have cried" - 10 stichera: Triodion on 6 and Menaion on 4, Glory, Both now: Theotokion of the Menaion. If there is a Doxastikon of the saint, then we select the Theotokion in His tone. (On Friday evening stichera: Idiomelon of the Triodion twice, Martyria of the Octoechos of the current tone - 4[^528] and of the Saint - 4, Glory: of the saint, if there is one, Both now: 1st Theotokion of the departing tone; if not, then Glory, Both now: the same Theotokion)
##### 4.1.8.4 Entrance with the Censer (on the Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner, on the 40 Martyrs and on Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday[^566] entrance - with the Gospel)
##### 4.1.8.5 At "O Gladsome Light" - Prokimenon of the Triodion, one reading of the Triodion and the second Prokimenon of the Triodion[^529]; then the Priest, having taken the triple-candle with the censer and exclaiming "Wisdom, Aright", begins "The Light of Christ", blessing with the triple-candle crosswise to the east, then, blessing to the west, says: "Illumines all"[^530]; all make three prostrations to the ground, and the Reader sings: "The Reading from Proverbs" and, when the Priest or Deacon exclaims: "Let us attend", reads the second reading of the Triodion
7.	"Let my prayer be set forth". Has after it three verses and is sung six times, that is: twice - before the first verse, once - after each verse and again once at the end. And among us the first time "Let my prayer be set forth" is sung by the Priest, the second time - by the Choir, all three verses - by the Priest, after each the Choir - "Let my prayer be set forth", the last time in a higher voice up to the half - the Priest, and from the half - the Choir. Where there are two choirs, they sing "Let my prayer be set forth" alternately: the first begins after the Priest, and the second half is sung by both choirs together. When the Priest sings, he himself stands, and the Choir and people kneel; when the Choir sings, the Choir stands together with the people, and the Priest kneels. If there are two choirs, the one singing stands, and the other kneels; people who are on the side of the right choir kneel, stand and sing with the right choir, and those on the left - with the left[^531].
The Priest sings "Let my prayer be set forth" first in front of the altar, then the 1st verse - on the south, the 2nd - on the west, the 3rd - on the north side, and at the end the first half - again in front of the holy table[^532]. He censes each side of the altar twice, that is before the beginning of his singing and at the end of the Choir's singing, that is at "The lifting up of my hands", for then, having censed, he moves to the next side of the altar, in order to cense there also, so that the Choir is ready to sing its verse[^533].
##### 4.1.8.6 Immediately after the end of the singing of "Let my prayer be set forth" is exclaimed "Let us all say" and according to the order - Liturgy of the Presanctified
Notes
##### 4.1.8.7 If there is no Liturgy of the Presanctified, then we sing Vespers according to the rubric of Monday, Tuesday and Thursday evening, that is from the 10 stichera on "Lord, I have cried" we transfer the first 3 to the Aposticha, and the other 6 we sing on "Lord, I have cried"[^534]; and there will be on Wednesday stichera of the Triodion - 3 and of the Menaion - 3, and on Friday Martyria of the Octoechos - 3 and of the Menaion - 3.
