On Monday of the second week after the Descent
### 4.2.22 Beginning of the Fast of the Saints Chief Apostles Peter and Paul
This fast begins on Monday after the Sunday of All Saints, and ends on the Eve of the feast of the Holy Apostles. Regarding the divine service on this, first, Monday, if a saint with Polyeleos does not fall, "Alleluia" was sung once and the whole church rule was Lenten, as on Lenten days of Great Lent; however, now we limit "Alleluia" to Great Lent, and here we sing "God is the Lord".
Regarding the fast; the Synod of Lviv limits it to Mondays, Wednesdays and Fridays of each of its weeks and permits also dairy products; on the other four days it permits meat, however, on the condition of the 50th Psalm for the clergy, and 5 "Our Father" and 5 "Virgin Theotokos" for the laity, before lunch and before supper[^660].
On Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints
### 4.2.23 Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist
Transferred to the second Sunday after the Descent.
This feast lasts eight days: from Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints to the next [Thursday], on which it is leavetaken. But since, according to our custom, it is transferred from Thursday to the following Sunday, the Synod of Lviv orders to sing the service of this feast on Sunday, with All-Night Vigil, exactly as given in the Triodion on Thursday of the feast itself, according to the general rubric of a feast of the Lord of the first rank, without the Sunday [service]; and on the days preceding and following this Sunday, to sing it according to the general rubric in the middle of a feast of the Lord. Besides this, the Synod orders that the procession with the Holy Mysteries take place on the Sunday itself, after the Prayer behind the Ambo, as usual, that is from the church to four altars, erected outside the church and equally distant from one another. Where this would be impossible, it will be sufficient to begin the procession from the altar in the church and, having gone around the church three times, to end this rite on the same altar in the church with the blessing with the Holy Mysteries. Regarding the four readings of the Gospel, which are usually sung at each of the four altars, the Synod orders to choose those in which there is mention of the Most Holy Mysteries, according to the rubric prescribed for this feast[^661].
After this two rubrics must be given: 1. How on this feast the Exposition of the Holy Mysteries takes place; 2. How the procession with the Holy Mysteries takes place.
RUBRIC I
ON THE EXPOSITION OF THE HOLY MYSTERIES
#### 4.2.23.1 AT THE LITURGY
The Priest, on one of the days preceding the feast, let him consecrate at the Liturgy two Lambs: one let him consume at Communion, and the other let him place in the tabernacle and keep until the day of the feast. All this let him do according to the rubric given for Great Thursday here, on page 385, in the note. On the day of the feast itself, after the Proskomedia, immediately before the beginning of the Divine Liturgy, after the censing of everything as usual by deacons (or by the Priest himself), the exposition of the Holy Mysteries takes place in the following way: the first deacon (who is on the right of the Priest) places the Gospel on the right side of the holy table, unlocks the artophorion and, having bowed low to the Holy Mysteries, takes from the artophorion the pyx, all bend their knees, and he places it on the folded antimension; if there be a monstrance, then he unlocks the pyx, takes from it the Lamb, not touching it with his fingers, but holding by the stand on which it lies, places it into the groove of the monstrance, locks it well, then places the pyx into the artophorion, bows before it and, having locked the artophorion, places the monstrance, turned with face side to the people, on the antimension and steps aside a little to the right side. The Priest puts incense into the censer, blesses it, saying: "Blessed is our God", and censes with both hands the Holy Mysteries three times, bowing His head at each censing. Then, having taken upon Himself with the help of the second deacon the omophorion, approaches the holy table and, having bowed low, with the help of the first Deacon takes with both hands with the omophorion the monstrance and, having turned it with the reverse side to himself, turns his right side to the people and sings solemnly and majestically the beginning of the first strophe: "O saving Victim", and all people continue to sing the same strophe. And again the Priest - the beginning of the second strophe: "To the One God in Trinity", and all sing the rest. The Priest signs the people crosswise with the Holy Mysteries, then goes in a semicircle from the southern side to the northern and, having returned by the northern to the altar, places the monstrance on the antimension, having turned it with the help of the Deacon with face side to the people. Then, having stepped aside a little to the left, descends before the steps of the altar so as not to turn his back to the Holy Mysteries, kneels and, having put aside the omophorion and having censed again three times the Holy Mysteries, as at the beginning, waits, kneeling, until the Deacon, having approached the middle of the altar and bowed low, places the Holy Mysteries on the tabernacle. Then the Priest, having ascended the steps, begins the Liturgy from "Blessed is the Kingdom", "O Heavenly King" and the rest. During the Liturgy the sacred ministers never turn their backs to the Holy Mysteries, as our Sluzhebnyky prescribe: "When (the Priest), according to the ritual prescriptions, has to turn to the people, he should not turn from the middle of the altar, so as not to turn his back to the Body of Christ, but, having bowed low and stepped aside a little to the left side, from this side turns to the people and, having turned again to the altar, bows low to the Body of Christ, and then, when the Priest has to make a full turn, as at 'Always, now and ever', when he turns with the Holy Mysteries, then he does not make a full turn, so as not to turn his back to the Body of Christ"[^662]. Let us add also that when the Priest or deacon goes out before the iconostasis for censing, he does not cense from the middle, nor turns his back to the Holy Mysteries, but stands a little on the side, the Priest - from the side of the Theotokos, and the deacons: one - from the side of the Savior, and the other - of the Theotokos.
Notes
##### 4.2.23.1.1 If there be no monstrance, then the Holy Mysteries are exposed in the pyx
##### 4.2.23.1.2 After the cleansing of the chalice the Priest does not place the Gospel on the antimension, since after the Prayer behind the Ambo the Holy Mysteries will have to be placed on the altar
##### 4.2.23.1.3 If there be no deacons, then the Priest does all this himself
RUBRIC II
ON THE PROCESSION WITH THE HOLY MYSTERIES AFTER THE PRAYER BEHIND THE AMBO
After the conclusion of the Prayer behind the Ambo the sacred ministers stand before the steps of the altar, and the first deacon ascends the steps and, having bowed low, takes down the Holy Mysteries from the tabernacle, places on the folded antimension and steps aside a little to his side. All kneel, and the Priest, having censed three times the Holy Mysteries and having taken upon himself the omophorion, ascends the steps, with the help of the Deacon takes with both hands the Holy Mysteries, turns to the people, as at the beginning of the Liturgy, and, having begun the troparion "Bread transubstantiated" or some other pious hymn, preceded by two deacons with censers, enters under the canopy and proceeds to the church doors to the sounds of bells and the procession from the church to the first altar begins, and the Choir and people continue to sing the troparion or something else. The order of the procession is such. In front goes the cross-bearer between two candle-bearers, then banner- and icon-bearers, then four girls with icons, after them the church brotherhood, two by two, with candles, choirs and all the clergy, two by two, with candles, according to their dignity: the nearest priests - in phelonia, and others - in epitrachelia, the last - the main priest with the Holy Mysteries, preceded by two deacons, who from the sides unceasingly cense, and candle-bearers surrounding the canopy. When the procession arrives at the first altar and stops near it, all the clergy divides into two wings, the canopy is placed opposite the altar, and the Priest with the Holy Mysteries ascends to the altar and, with the help of the Deacon, places the Holy Mysteries on the folded antimension; then, having bent his knees, censes them three times and, having risen, steps aside a little to the left and turns to the people. Then the Gospel-bearers approach the Priest, the Priest opens the Gospel at the place of the 1st reading, and the first Deacon exclaims: "And that we may be accounted worthy" etc., choir responds "Lord, have mercy" (3), priest sings: "Peace be to all"; second deacon: "Wisdom, Aright. Let us listen" etc., priest: "The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to (Name)" and, when the first Deacon exclaims: "Let us attend", the Priest reads the first Gospel (Matthew, Section 108. 17-30). After the conclusion of the reading he kisses its beginning and, having bent his knees before the Holy Mysteries, sings (if there is a custom) with other sacred ministers the first member of the supplicatory song "Holy God" three times and all people - the same three times. Then, having again censed the Holy Mysteries three times and risen, bows low, takes the Holy Mysteries with both hands and, having turned to the right to the people blesses them crosswise once (bishop - three times), saying quietly: "Save, O God, Thy people and bless Thine inheritance", and so, to the singing of the festal troparion or other hymn, the Priest enters under the canopy and the procession moves to the second altar, from it - to the third, then - to the fourth, and at each everything takes place exactly as at the first, only the members of the supplicatory song and the readings of the Gospels are changed, of which the second will be from Mark, Section 64.12-26; the third - from Luke, Section 108.7-22; the fourth - from John, Section 23.48-58. If there be several priests, they read the Gospels alternately. After this the procession returns to the church and the Priest, having placed the Holy Mysteries on the altar, kneels, censes them three times, rises and, having taken the Holy Mysteries and turned to the people, sings solemnly and majestically the verse of the Ambrosian hymn: "Save Thy people, O Lord" (3), and the people repeat the first and second time the same, and when the Priest sings it the third time and finishes with the words "and bless Thine inheritance" blessing the people once, then the people sing the further verses of this hymn, and the Priest, having turned with the northern side to the altar and placed the Holy Mysteries on it, descends before the steps, puts aside the omophorion, kneels and censes them three times. Then the right deacon approaches the Holy Mysteries, bows low, takes from the tabernacle the pyx and, having taken the Lamb from the monstrance, places it with the stand into the pyx, and places it into the tabernacle and, having again bowed, locks the tabernacle and places the Gospel on the antimension. If there be no deacon, then the Priest himself does all this. Then all rise, and the Choir sings "Blessed be the name of the Lord" and the rest of the Liturgy, as usual.
Notes
##### 4.2.23.1.4 In case it is impossible to have four altars, see what the Synod of Lviv prescribes here, on page 448
##### 4.2.23.1.5 The Lamb placed in the tabernacle, let the Priest consume on the next day at the Liturgy together with the other, consecrated at this Liturgy: first - the consecrated, and then - the presanctified
##### 4.2.23.1.6 On Sunday evening at Vespers we sing the Great Prokimenon of the feast, given in the Triodion on the previous Thursday evening
On Thursday of the third week after the Descent
Apodosis of the feast of the Eucharist
Takes place according to the general rule of the Apodosis of a feast of the Lord.
On Friday after the Apodosis of the Eucharist
### 4.2.24 Feast of the Co-suffering of the Most Holy Theotokos
We celebrate this feast as one day, without All-Night Vigil, but only with Polyeleos.
At Great Vespers: "Blessed is the man". On "Lord, I have cried" - 6 stichera; after the Prokimenon of the day - 3 readings of the feast and the rest of the service - only of the feast, according to the rubric of the service of July 2 here, on page 305. Litiya, given in the Triodion, without All-Night Vigil we do not serve now.
At Great Matins: Canon one with heirmos on 12.
At the Liturgy: at the 3rd Antiphon, at "O come, let us worship", if Thou wishest, refrain the refrain: "Through the prayers of the Theotokos, who sing to Thee", as in the Pochaiv Triodion.

## 5.2 Chapter II: Temple Rubrics
which enter into the composition of our divine service
These rubrics are either general or specific. General [ones] are applicable to many cases, specific [ones] - only to some. We shall present first the general rubrics, and then - the specific.
### 5.2.1 General Rubrics
[^665]1.	On a temple feast, even if it be of a saint of a lower rank, an All-Night Vigil must be celebrated. So we do, and so holds the Tserkovne Oko in Chapter 4, concerning temples[^666].
##### 5.2.1.1 The feast of a temple saint, even if of a lower rank, exceeds even a feast of a saint with a Vigil, primarily in that, when it falls on a Sunday, it includes at Matins before the Canon the Prokimenon, the Gospel, and the Sunday Sticheron [after the Gospel], exactly as a Feast of the Theotokos; also, the Gospel Sticheron is transferred from the "Praises" to the end of Matins. Besides this, if it falls on a weekday, it excludes at the Liturgy the Apostle and Gospel of the day, which is then read on the day before the feast. Finally, the temple feast of a saint has its Apodosis, which takes place on the very day of the feast at Vespers for the following day, with an Entrance, as on a Feast of the Lord. In this Apodosis at "Lord, I have cried," there will be 6 stichera: 3 of the temple and 3 of the daily saint; Glory: of the temple; Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday [*Octoechos*] (Dogmatic) according to the tone of the Doxastikon. If the temple is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, then also Glory, Both now: of the temple. On Friday evening for Saturday, we always sing the Dogmatic of the tone that is being given away. After the Prokimenon - "Let us all say." Aposticha of the temple with its verses, Glory: of the temple, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday [*Octoechos*], according to the tone of the Doxastikon. If the temple is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, then Both now: of the temple. At the end: Troparion of the temple, Glory: Troparion of the daily saint, Glory, Both now: of the temple. Great Dismissal with the commemoration of the temple and the daily saint. At this Vespers, the service of the temple saint concludes; however, a temple saint does not have an Apodosis if the following day falls on a Sunday, or a Polyeleos saint
##### 5.2.1.2 If the feast of a temple saint falls in the midst of a feast, that is, during the Forefeast, Afterfeast, or Apodosis of a feast - observe the rubric of a Saint with Vigil in the Afterfeast, whether on a Sunday or on a weekday here, on pp. 104-105 [→REF:p104-105][^667], only on Sunday, at Matins, we sing the Prokimenon, Gospel and Sticheron of the temple; at the Praises - Glory: of the temple, and the Gospel Sticheron - after the dismissal of Matins[^668]
##### 5.2.1.3 On the very day of the feast, after the Ambo [prayer], in our lands, a Blessing of Water usually takes place outside the church, to where a procession goes: in front - the cross-bearer, then - the censer-bearer, the Deacon with the Gospel, and the Priest with a hand cross. It befits the church servers to carry there also the boat with incense, the sprinkler with the holy water vessel, a glass [cup], and a towel, so that the cross may be wiped after immersing it in the water, as well as the hands of the Priest. After the dismissal of the Liturgy, the Polychronion is sung for the Ecumenical Pontiff, the Emperor, the Metropolitan, one's own Bishop, all present sacred ministers, and all people of the parish
##### 5.2.1.4 On the day following the feast, according to local custom, a Liturgy for the deceased of the parish with a Parastas is sung, at which it is not fitting to take anything of the feast, but everything only - for the deceased
##### 5.2.1.5 The feast of a temple saint is usually transferred among us to the following Sunday; in throne [cathedral] temples - it is celebrated on its own day

--- Match 32 [Terms: Monastery] ---
NUMBER OF ALL-NIGHT VIGILS FOR THE WHOLE YEAR ACCORDING TO THE TYPIKON OF SAVA[^745]
Chapter 3.
We perform an All-Night Vigil on every Sunday, so that the total number of vigils for the whole year is 52. Vigils also take place on the Lord's Feasts in each month as follows:
September: 8, 14, 26.
November: 21.
December: 25.
January: 6, 17.
February: 2.
March: 25.
May: 8.
June: 24, 29.
August: 6, 15, 24 (likely 29).
If the Rector Wishes:
November: 13.
December: 5.
January: 20.
This is according to the usage of the Monastery of the Saint [Sabbas], where it happens necessarily. Litiya is also performed, if the Rector wishes.

--- Match 33 [Terms: monasteries] ---
RUBRIC
## 5.5 Rubrics Concerning the Holy Doors and Curtain of the Iconostasis
According to the rubric of the Greek Euchologion, the holy doors are opened[^782] only εις την αρχήν του μεγάλου Εσπερινού, οτε θυμια μονός ο Ιερεύς, εις τας εισόδους πασάς, ήγουν των Εσπερινών και της Λειτουργίας, και του αγίου Ευαγγελίου. Ωσαύτως ανοίγεται και από του Μετά φόβου... μέχρι της συμπληρώσεως της θείας Λειτουργίας.
The curtain, according to the present custom of Athonite monasteries, at the Liturgy, after the completion of the Proskomedia, is drawn open, and at the "Great Entrance" is drawn shut; at "Especially" it is drawn open, and at "And grant that with one mouth" it is drawn shut; at "With fear" it is drawn open and so remains until the conclusion of the Liturgy[^751]. According to the former custom of the Athonite Mountain, when one priest serves, that is without a Deacon, the curtain is drawn shut at the "Great Entrance" and is not drawn open until the end of communion. Besides this, it remains drawn open at Vespers and at Matins from the 1st Hour and at the Moleben. When the Hours are celebrated without Liturgy, it is drawn open for the reading of the Apostle and drawn shut after the dismissal of the "Typika"[^752].
The End and Glory to God.
Bless, O All-Holy Theotokos, this work for the good of God's holy Churches. Amen.

--- Match 34 [Terms: ancient custom, parish] ---
> **Deacon**: "Let us pray to the Lord."
 
> **Choir**: "Lord, have mercy."
 The Priest exclaims: "For Holy Art Thou, our God." The Deacon censes the holy table from the four sides and everything else, according to custom (see no. 33). 
> **Choir**: "Let everything that has breath praise the Lord."
 The Deacon recites both verses, and the Choir repeats: "Let everything that has breath," after which the 
> **Deacon**: "And that we may be vouchsafed."
 
> **Choir**: "Lord, have mercy"
 (3). The Priest takes the Gospel, goes out before the holy doors, stands at a slight distance before them on the solea, places the Gospel on the analogion and reads it, turned to the people. 
> **Deacon**: "Wisdom, arise, let us hear the Holy Gospel,"
 and the Priest blesses: "Peace be to all." 
> **Choir**: "And to Thy spirit."
 
> **Priest**: "The reading from the Holy Gospel according to (Name)."
 
> **Deacon**: "Let us be attentive."
 The Priest reads the Matins Gospel.
##### 6.1.4.1.6 After the reading, the Priest kisses the closed book and carries it to the middle of the temple, where he places it on the tetrapod, and he himself returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary. The Deacon reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," if it is Sunday, or the Reader - the 50th Psalm "Have mercy on me, O God."
After the sticheron, the Deacon [is] at his usual place: "O God, save Thy people." 
> **Choir**: "Lord, have mercy"
 (12) and the Priest exclaims: "By the grace and compassions." 
> **Choir**: "Amen."
 The Canon begins. The holy doors, if there was no Vigil, are closed.
##### 6.1.4.1.7 If there was a Vigil, the Priest after the exclamation "By the grace," holding the cross in his hands, goes out through the holy [doors], and the Deacon with the oil, blessed at Vespers, and the anointing brush, - through the northern doors and they stand on the right side of the tetrapod. There the Priest anoints crosswise the forehead of everyone who approaches to kiss the Holy Gospel. After the completion of the anointing [Myrovanne] they return to the sanctuary: the Priest - through the holy, and the Deacon - through the southern doors and, having closed the holy doors, the Priest takes off the phelonion
##### 6.1.4.1.8 After the third and sixth Odes, the Deacon sings the Small Litany either before the holy doors, or near the tetrapod, if the anointing was not yet finished, and the Priest exclaims the exclamation either from the sanctuary, or from the tetrapod
After the singing of the Katavasia of the eighth Ode, the Deacon takes the censer and, having stood before the icon of the Theotokos, says: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light let us magnify in songs" and, having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, censes the holy table from four sides and everything else, according to the prescription (see no. 33).
After the ninth Ode and after the recitation by the Choir of "It is truly meet," if it is not Sunday, but if it is Sunday or a feast, after the singing of the Heirmos, the Deacon exclaims from his place the Small Litany. The Priest exclaims: "For all the powers of heaven praise Thee." The Choir sings the Exaposteilarion.
##### 6.1.4.1.9 After the Praises stichera, at "Glory," the Deacon opens the holy doors. The Priest in the phelonion stands before the throne, the Deacon stands to his right and, after the completion of the last sticheron, the Priest, turned to the east, exclaims: "Glory to Thee, Who Hast shown us the Light." The Great Doxology is sung. Then, also according to custom, the Troparion is recited, and the Priest, preceded by the Deacon, carries the Holy Gospel from the tetrapod into the sanctuary and places it on the holy table
##### 6.1.4.1.10 After this, the Deacon stands at his usual place and exclaims the litanies "Have mercy on us, O God" and "Let us complete." The Priest blesses the people and exclaims: "Peace be to all."
> **Choir**: "And to Thy spirit."
 
> **Deacon**: "Bow your heads unto the Lord."
 
> **Choir**: "To Thee, O Lord."
 The Priest recites quietly the prayer "O Holy Lord," after which He exclaims: "For Thine it is." The dismissal follows, which the Deacon begins: "Wisdom" (see no. 36).
##### 6.1.4.1.11 After the dismissal, the Reader immediately begins the 1st Hour: "Come, let us worship" (3) and the psalms. After "Our Father," the Priest in the epitrachelion: "For Thine is the kingdom." Reader - Kontakion and "Lord, have mercy" (40). The Priest goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors and before the holy [doors], turned to the east, loudly: "Thou Who at all times"
> **Reader**: "Lord, have mercy"
 (3), "Glory, Both now", "More honorable than the Cherubim", "In the name of the Lord, bless, father." 
> **Priest**: "God be merciful to us."
 
> **Reader**: "Amen."
 Priest recites the prayer "O Christ, the True Light", "Glory to Thee, O Christ God." 
> **Reader**: "Glory, Both now"
, "Lord, have mercy" (3), "Bless." The Priest imparts the Small Dismissal, returns into the sanctuary and takes off the epitrachelion.
#### 6.1.4.2 In Concelebration of Two Deacons
##### 6.1.4.2.1 At the end of the Six Psalms, the 1st deacon goes out through the northern doors and exclaims the Great Litany, as well as the verses at "God is the Lord" and, having made a small bow, returns into the sanctuary through the southern doors
After the first Kathisma the 2nd deacon, and after the second Kathisma the 1st deacon, sing the Small Litany.
After the Troparia "The Angelic Council" or after the Magnifications, the 2nd deacon likewise sings the Small Litany.
After the Gradual [Gradual], the 
> **First Deacon**: "Let us be attentive."
 
> **Priest**: "Peace be to all."
 
> **Second Deacon**: "Wisdom, let us be attentive."
 
> **First Deacon**: "Let us pray to the Lord."
 Both deacons cense the holy table, the iconostasis, the choirs and the people, as noted in the rubric of Vespers in concelebration with two deacons (see no. 40). At "Let everything that has breath," the 1st deacon recites the first verse, and the 2nd deacon - the second.
##### 6.1.4.2.2 For the anointing of the faithful, the 1st deacon goes out with the Priest, and the 2nd exclaims the Small Litany after the third Ode, and also - after the sixth Ode
After the Katavasia of the 8th Ode, the 1st Deacon takes the censer, goes out through the northern doors, stands before the icon of the Theotokos and calls: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," then returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary and, together with the 2nd deacon, censes the throne and everything else, as noted above (see no. 39), and after the censing they return into the sanctuary: the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern doors.
##### 6.1.4.2.3 After the ninth Ode, the 1st Deacon exclaims the Small Litany
##### 6.1.4.2.4 After the Praises stichera, the holy doors are opened. The 1st deacon stands before the holy table to the right of the Priest, and the 2nd - to the left. After the Great Doxology, all, having made a small bow, go to the tetrapod, the Priest takes the Holy Gospel and, preceded by the deacons, returns into the sanctuary
##### 6.1.4.2.5 After this, the deacons go out: the 1st Deacon exclaims the first litany "Have mercy on us, O God," and the 2nd - the second "Let us complete."
> **First Deacon**: "Bow your heads unto the Lord."
 The dismissal follows, which the 1st deacon begins: "Wisdom" (see no. 36, note).
#### 6.1.4.3 Without a Deacon
##### 6.1.4.3.1 A Priest who serves Matins without a Deacon does and says everything that belongs to the Deacon, considering this:
##### 6.1.4.3.2 After the completion of the Six Psalms, He does not return into the sanctuary, but, standing before the holy doors, exclaims the Great Litany and its exclamation: "For to Thee belongs," as well as the verses during the singing by the Choir of "God is the Lord." After the completion of this, He makes a small bow and returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary
It should be noted that when the Priest does not have enough time to recite all the Matins Prayers before the iconostasis, he may finish them in the sanctuary during the reading of the Kathismata.
After the Prokimenon, He Himself exclaims: "Let us pray to the Lord"; reads the verses at "Let everything that has breath"; before the reading of the Gospel, exclaims: "Let us be attentive," and after the Gospel reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ."
After the 3rd and 6th Odes of the Canon - Small Litanies and exclaims from the throne or from the place where he anoints.
After the anointing, he returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary and takes off the phelonion.
##### 6.1.4.3.3 After the Katavasia of the 8th Ode, he goes out in the epitrachelion through the northern doors, stands before the icon of the Theotokos and calls: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," then censes, as noted above (see no. 33). After the censing, he returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary, exclaims the Small Litany and its exclamation
##### 6.1.4.3.4 After the Praises stichera, at "Glory," He puts on the phelonion, opens the holy doors and, after the last sticheron, exclaims: "Glory to Thee, Who Hast shown us the Light." Everything else takes place as was presented (see nos. 82-83)
##### 6.1.4.3.5 After the dismissal, the Priest closes the holy doors, takes off the phelonion and the 1st Hour begins
#### 6.1.4.4 In Concelebration of Priests
##### 6.1.4.4.1 Concelebrants, in an even number, put on epitrachelia over their ryasas and put on phelons during the reading of the Kathisma and stand on the sides of the holy table, as at Vespers (see no. 50). During the singing of the Polyeleos, all go out, having made a small bow, through the holy doors, having lowered the phelonion and hands down, in front - the younger ones. Two first ones carry the icon of the feast or saint, if it has not yet been placed on the left side of the tetrapod between both choirs, and place it on the tetrapod; all stand on the sides of the tetrapod in two rows, facing one another, the principal celebrant stands in the middle before the tetrapod, and to his right - the Deacon
##### 6.1.4.4.2 Then all sing the Magnification. When the Choir repeats the Magnification, the Priest, preceded by the Deacon with a lighted candle, censes from four sides the icon of the feast or saint on the tetrapod, the holy table, the sanctuary, the iconostasis, the concelebrants, both choirs, the people and once more the icon of the feast, but only from the front. If, however, the usual censing takes place before the Gospel, then at the Magnification the Priest censes only the icon itself
> **Choir**: "Glory"
 and again the Magnification. Then the celebrant and concelebrants sing the Magnification once more.
##### 6.1.4.4.3 After the Magnifications all, the younger ones - in front, return through the holy doors into the sanctuary before the holy table. After the Gospel all, having made a small bow, depart and take off their vestments
#### 6.1.4.5 Shortening of Matins[^767]
##### 6.1.4.5.1 Beginning
- The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion, censes as usual (see at "Lord, I have cried," no. 75).
- Then he exclaims: "Glory to the Holy," and the Choir recites everything else and the 142nd Psalm on Sunday or the regular one - on feasts.
- After the Great Litany - "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Sunday or Feast, Theotokion according to the tone of the Troparion.
##### 6.1.4.5.2 Sung Kathismata
- Regular Kathismata with Sessional Hymns are not taken, but sung ones are taken thus: "The Angelic Council" - only on Sundays, "Praise the name of the Lord" (Polyeleos) - only on feasts, "By the waters of Babylon" - only on the Sundays of Great Lent.
- The Priest at the appointed time puts on the phelonion, opens the holy doors and exclaims: "Peace be to all." Then he censes. If there was censing at the Magnification, then before the Reading of the Gospel there will be no censing.
- Prokimenon, "Let everything that has breath" - according to the tone of the season.
##### 6.1.4.5.3 Bringing out the Gospel
- The Priest, having read the Gospel, carries the Holy Book out to the tetrapod, accompanied by two candle-bearers.
- The Reader, if there is no deacon, reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ."
- All go to kiss the Gospel. If there was a Litiya, then the anointing takes place (see no. 80).
- Having returned to the throne, the Priest recites the prayer "O God, save." After this He does not close the holy doors in view of the shortening of Matins.
##### 6.1.4.5.4 Magnification of the Theotokos
- Having recited the prayer "O God, save," the Priest takes the censer and goes out before the icon of the Theotokos.
- There he exclaims: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," censes the icon, then returns into the sanctuary and censes everything, as usual.
- The Choir sings "More honorable than the Cherubim" at the beginning and at the end of the verses, which both choirs recite alternately.
##### 6.1.4.5.5 Laudatory Prayers
- Psalms 148, 149 and 150 are sung singly, in turn.
- After "Most Blessed" the Priest exclaims: "Glory to Thee, Who Hast shown us the Light." At the Great Doxology there will be no censing.
- During the singing of the Troparion, the Priest carries the Gospel back, and after the completion - immediately says the dismissal.
#### 6.1.4.6 Beginning of Paschal Matins
At Paschal Matins the censing takes place with special solemnity. The servers, having put on the best vestments, as at the Divine Liturgy, go out before the throne in the usual order and make a bow all together. The principal celebrant gives the Gospel to the 1st server, and to the 2nd - the icon of the Resurrection of Christ, He Himself takes the cross with His left hand, and with the right (from the Deacon) - the censer. He censes first the throne, and afterwards - the servers. Then the principal celebrant, having given the censer to the Deacon, stands at his place. All bow before the throne and turn to the east to go out to the narthex. The exit takes place according to the usual order of seniority under the peeling of bells and with the singing of the hymn "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior." And the order is such: candle-bearers, priests, deacons with lighted candles, and behind them - the principal celebrant. All are also given lighted candles. In the narthex all stand before the closed church doors.
Here the principal celebrant takes the censer from the Deacon and censes the icons of the narthex, the kliroses and the people. The Deacon precedes him the entire time with a lighted candle. Then the principal celebrant returns before the doors and censes the Deacon; and then the latter, having taken the censer from the principal celebrant, censes him and immediately gives the censer back to the principal celebrant, who thrice censes the doors and then exclaims: "Glory to the Holy." After "Amen," the principal celebrant with the concelebrants sings thrice "Christ is risen" and the verses "Let God arise," to which the people respond with the song "Christ is risen." After the last "Christ is risen," the principal celebrant opens the doors (Among us he knocks thrice with the cross) and enters the church, and behind him all the servers and people (see no. 71; *Liturgicon*, pp. 141-146).
### 6.1.5 Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil
of St. John Chrysostom and St. Basil the Great
#### 6.1.5.1 In Concelebration of One Deacon
##### 6.1.5.1.1 According to the ancient custom of the Eastern Church, only one Holy Liturgy may be celebrated at the same holy table. However, one may depart from this rule if the Divine Liturgy must be repeated for the benefit of the faithful. If there is one or several secondary churches in the parish, then the more solemn Liturgy is celebrated in the main sanctuary, and other liturgies should be celebrated rather in the side sanctuaries
##### 6.1.5.1.2 The Eucharistic bread does not absolutely have to be baked on the day the Holy Sacrifice is offered. The same bread can be kept for several days and used at several liturgies, provided that all the ceremonies of the Proskomedia are observed over it or over a part of it, cutting the bread and removing the Lamb and particles according to the prescription. Also, it is not necessary to always use five prosphora, if the Lamb and all particles can be taken from one and the same prosphora. The custom of using five prosphora may be kept, if one pleases, when celebrating more solemn liturgies (see no. 179)
##### 6.1.5.1.3 The Priest who wishes to serve the Sacrament must first of all be reconciled with all and hold nothing against anyone, keep his heart as far as possible from evil thoughts, be continent already from the evening and keep the fast until the very time of the service and prepare himself for the offering of the sacrifice by prayers (see no. 190)
##### 6.1.5.1.4 At the prescribed time, the Priest enters the church and, together with the Deacon, bows three times before the holy doors to the east
Then the Deacon says in a low voice: "Bless, Master." 
> **Priest**: "Blessed is our God."
 
> **Deacon**: "Amen."

--- Match 35 [Terms: local custom] ---
The Priest follows the Deacon, takes from him the holy chalice and from the holy doors blesses the people with it: "O God, save," and both return to the holy table.
If there be faithful for communion, the Deacon, having placed the spoon into the chalice, covers it with the purificator or its veil, or both and, having made a small bow, receives it with piety from the Priest, approaches the holy doors and, lifting the holy chalice, shows it to the people, saying: "With fear of God and with faith draw near." 
> **Choir**: "Blessed is He that comes."
 The Priest follows the Deacon, takes from him the holy chalice and recites the prayer "I believe, O Lord." Simultaneously the Deacon takes the diskos from the throne. After the completion of the prayer, the Priest uncovers the holy chalice, holds the veil and purificator with his left hand and communicates with the spoon, saying "Of the precious, most holy." The Deacon holds the diskos under the chin of the communicant. After communion the Priest covers the chalice with the purificator or veil, or both, leaving the spoon in the chalice, blesses the people with it: "O God, save" and both return to the holy table.
One may also do thus: the Deacon covers the holy chalice with the purificator or its veil and, having made a small bow, receives piously the chalice from the Priest, approaches the holy doors and, lifting the chalice, shows it to the people, saying: "With fear of God and with faith draw near." 
> **Choir**: "Blessed is He that comes."
 The Deacon returns to the holy table, where he places the chalice, from which he removes the small veil. The Priest takes the holy chalice with the spoon and purificator, and the Deacon - the diskos and they go out of the sanctuary; those who wish to communicate approach. After the prayer "I believe, O Lord," which the Priest leads, the faithful, with all piety and fear, having folded their arms crosswise on their chest, make a small bow and one by one approach for communion, or receive it in another manner, according to local custom. The Priest, communicating, says to each communicant: "The precious, most holy."
After the completion of the communion of the faithful, the Priest leaves the spoon in the chalice and covers it with the purificator. Then, having returned with the Deacon into the sanctuary, he places the Holy Gifts on the holy table, and the Deacon covers the holy chalice with its veil. The Priest takes once more the holy chalice and from the holy doors blesses the people with it, exclaiming: "O God, save Thy people." 
> **Choir**: "We have seen the true light."
 And both return to the holy table.
##### 6.1.5.1.47 The Priest places the holy chalice on the throne and censes it three times, praying quietly: "Be Thou exalted, O God, above the heavens." Then He gives the censer to the Deacon and, having taken the holy diskos, places it on the Deacon's head, and the Deacon, holding it with piety in front and looking towards the holy doors, saying nothing, goes straight between the holy table and the holy doors to the table of oblation and places the holy diskos on it. After this he waits for the Priest at the same place. And the Priest, having made a small bow and taken the holy chalice, turned to the holy doors, looks at the people and says quietly: "Blessed is our God," and then exclaims: "Always, now and ever."
> **Choir**: "Amen. Let our mouths be filled."
 The Priest goes to the table of oblation, after which the Deacon censes the Holy Gifts and the Priest. The Priest, having placed the Holy Gifts and made a small bow, returns to the holy table.
##### 6.1.5.1.48 The Deacon, having untied the orarion, as custom prescribes, goes out through the northern doors and, standing at the usual place, exclaims: "Arise! Having partaken of the divine."
The Priest recites quietly the Prayer of Thanksgiving "We thank Thee, O Master" and, having made with the Holy Gospel the sign of the cross over the folded antimension [iliton][^779], places it on the antimension [iliton]. Exclamation: "For Thou Art our sanctification." 
> **Choir**: "Amen."

--- Match 36 [Terms: local custom] ---
The Priest follows the Deacon, takes from him the holy chalice and from the holy doors blesses the people with it: "O God, save," and both return to the holy table.
If there be faithful for communion, the Deacon, having placed the spoon into the chalice, covers it with the purificator or its veil, or both and, having made a small bow, receives it with piety from the Priest, approaches the holy doors and, lifting the holy chalice, shows it to the people, saying: "With fear of God and with faith draw near." 
> **Choir**: "Blessed is He that comes."

##### 6.1.5.2.20 The Priest places the holy chalice on the throne and censes it three times, praying quietly: "Be Thou exalted, O God, above the heavens." Then He gives the censer to the Deacon and, having taken the holy diskos, places it on the Deacon's head, and the Deacon, holding it with piety in front and looking towards the holy doors, saying nothing, goes straight between the holy table and the holy doors to the table of oblation and places the holy diskos on it. After this he waits for the Priest at the same place. And the Priest, having made a small bow and taken the holy chalice, turned to the holy doors, looks at the people and says quietly: "Blessed is our God," and then exclaims: "Always, now and ever."

 The Priest goes to the table of oblation, after which the Deacon censes the Holy Gifts and the Priest. The Priest, having placed the Holy Gifts and made a small bow, returns to the holy table.
##### 6.1.5.2.21 The Deacon, having untied the orarion, as custom prescribes, goes out through the northern doors and, standing at the usual place, exclaims: "Arise! Having partaken of the divine."
The Priest recites quietly the Prayer of Thanksgiving "We thank Thee, O Master" and, having made with the Holy Gospel the sign of the cross over the folded antimension [iliton][^779], places it on the antimension [iliton]. Exclamation: "For Thou Art our sanctification." 
> **Choir**: "Amen."

--- Match 37 [Terms: ancient custom] ---
6. Odes and Canons. The Biblical Odes, given at the end of the Psalter, are intertwined with the odes of the canon, as given: before the ode of the canon the corresponding ode of the Psalter is recited, leaving its last verses for refrains to the troparia of the canon, in such quantity as there will be troparia in the ode of the canon, including the heirmos, except two, for to the penultimate troparion we refrain "Glory", and to the last - "Both now". The 2nd Ode is recited in full before the ode of the canon, and to the troparia of its canon we refrain: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee". According to our ancient custom, the Odes of the Psalter are recited only in those odes in which the triodion occurs, and in the other odes we refrain the usual refrains to the troparia of the canon. The Greek Typikon prescribes singing the Odes of the Psalter in the tone of the *Menaion* canon, but now we do not even know what their melody was.

--- Match 38 [Terms: local custom] ---
^[14] Since no Typikon (the Synod testifies) prescribes censing in the temple here, it would be better if the deacons cense from the place of the Litiya when they arrive there. And since this is better, we have placed such a rubric in the text, and we have completely rejected the worse existing local custom of censing the Holy Table and the local icons as at "Lord, I have cried," although the Synod mentions it too. However, if there are icons in the narthex, it is proper to cense them first, for the ancient Typikons prescribe so.

--- Match 39 [Terms: ancient rule] ---
[259] More simply, according to the ancient rule, the cross with basil is placed on a tray. Today's Greeks more often, instead of basil, place flowers under the cross.

--- Match 40 [Terms: Monastic] ---
### Feast of St. Catherine (Transfer of Feasts)
- **Category:** *Liturgical Monastic & Parish Celebrations*
- **Source Reference:** *Footnote 279, L298*

--- Match 41 [Terms: monks] ---
[279] We find the Service of St. Catherine as having Polyeleos only in our Pochaiv *Menaion* and in the new Greek-Venetian one; but in others -- without Polyeleos. In later Greek typikons we find it given on 25 November with the Apodosis of the Feast of the Entry into the Temple of the Most Holy Theotokos, and in earlier ones -- on the 24th, as it is today among Greek-Slavs. Thus the Greek-Venetian *Menaion* of 1615 gives: Σημειωσαι ουν, ότι ευρομεν εν τισι Τυπικοις των νεωτέρων, ότι εν τιοα των πόλεων ψαλλεται η ακολουθία της αγίας μάρτυρος Αικατερίνης εν τη κε του μηνός, εν η και αποδιδοται η εορτή. Εν ετεροις δε Τυπικοις παλαιοτεροις ευρομεν αυτήν εορτάζεσαι εν τη κδ του αυτού μηνός: εν δε τη κε ψαλλομεν την ακολουθίαν του εν αγιοις Κλημεντος Παπα Ρώμης, και Πέτρου Αλεξανδρείας, εν η και αποδιδοται η εορτή των Εισοδιων /Note, therefore, that we found in some newer typikons, that in some places the service of the holy martyr Catherine is sung on the 25th day of this month, on which the feast is also given up. In other older typikons we found that her memory is celebrated on the 24th of this month, and on the 25th we sing the service of Saints Clement Pope of Rome and Peter of Alexandria, and on this day also the feast of the Entry is given up/. This transfer from the 24th to the 25th day, in the opinion of the Greeks, as explained by the new Greek-Venetian *Menaion* (24 November, p. 158), was done at the wish of the monks of Mount Sinai, so that it would not coincide with the Apodosis of the feast and, so that in this way the memory of the saint would be honored more solemnly. For we know that the body of the holy great martyr was transferred by angelic power to Mount Sinai and buried there.

- **Source Reference:** *Part 3 *Menaion*, L2387*

### Feasts of Saints (St. Peter and Paul stichera)
- **Category:** *Liturgical Monastic & Parish Celebrations*
- **Source Reference:** *Footnote 282, L301*

--- Match 46 [Terms: Monastic] ---
### Feasts of the Holy Apostles (Vigil and Polyeleos)
- **Category:** *Liturgical Monastic & Parish Celebrations*
- **Source Reference:** *Footnote 704, L725*

--- Match 47 [Terms: ancient custom] ---
[264] Ancient Greek typikons mention nothing about a deacon, neither at the bringing out, nor at the exaltation, nor at the laying of the precious Cross, but only about one priest. The Greek typikon, similarly to the Slavic ones, prescribes for the priest to vest in all priestly vestments: αλασσει ο Ιερεύς πασαν την ιερατικην στολην /the priest vests in all priestly vestments/. The Greek *Triodion* (Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross) and the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, similarly to Slavic typikons, prescribes for the priest to enter with the cross from the sanctuary to the temple through the northern doors: εκ του αριστερού Βήματος /from the left side of the sanctuary/. Greek typikons, with the exception of the new Constantinopolitan, and the Slavic of the Lviv University, prescribe for the priest, having exited from the sanctuary to the temple, to head to the entrance doors that lead from the temple to the narthex, which are sometimes called royal, and there await the end of the Doxology and from there exclaim "Wisdom, attend", and then head to the tetrapod, which is before the iconostasis. Although these doors are called royal, βασιλικαι /royal/, one must understand that they were called so in the past. Today we call royal the middle doors of the iconostasis, which according to ancient custom are called holy: αγιαι ΰυραι /holy doors/ (chapter 96, Greek Typikon).

--- Match 48 [Terms: Monastic] ---
### Holy Liturgy (Concelebration & Repetition)
- **Category:** *Liturgical Monastic & Parish Celebrations*
- **Source Reference:** *Point 97, Appendix, L275*

--- Match 49 [Terms: ancient custom, parish] ---
97. According to the ancient custom of the Eastern Church, only one Holy Liturgy may be celebrated at the same holy table. However, one may depart from this rule if the Divine Liturgy must be repeated for the benefit of the faithful. If there is one or several secondary churches in the parish, then the more solemn Liturgy is celebrated in the main sanctuary, and other liturgies should be celebrated rather in the side sanctuaries.

--- Match 50 [Terms: monasteries, In monasteries] ---
[77] When two kathismata should be read, and when three, we noted in the note to the Order of Daily Matins on page 41. Regarding small litanies which are read between kathismata and sessional hymns, the Lviv Synod, our Horologions and our Service Books (at the end) give them also at Small Matins, and they are used also by the Greek-Melkites (according to the testimony of their patriarch bl. m. Clement Bahous). Conversely, in the Russian Typikon, approved by their Governing Synod (Moscow edition of 1859), in the Order of Daily Matins it is written that the priest says them only at the Vigil and when the memory of saints having a Polyeleos and Doxology is celebrated; similarly also in the new Wallachian-Bucharest Typikon, in which in the Order of Daily Matins is given: "We take sessional hymns from the *Octoechos* without litanies" (untranslatable); and adds: "In monasteries, usually, between kathismata litanies are taken only on Sunday and when more special saints occur". From the Greek Typikon no conclusion can be made, for in it small litanies are not mentioned neither at Small Matins, nor at Great, nor before the Canon, nor after the Canon. It is assumed in the Typikon that this is known to everyone. From the rubrics of the Greek *Triodion*, given at Matins on Meatfare Saturday, one can conclude that these litanies should be said also at Small Matins. For it gives: η συνηιϊης στιχολογια. Εν δε ταις συναπταις πασαις της στιχολογιας και του Κανόνος, μνημονεύονται πάντες οι προαπελΰοντες Πατέρες και αδελφοί ημων /Ordinary Stichology. In all small litanies of the Stichology and of the Canon, all our fathers and brethren who have passed away before us are commemorated/. Let everyone observe the rubric and custom of his Church. Finally, in our opinion, later typikons reject litanies here only for the sake of greater shortening of the divine service; if the single difference between Small and Great Matins were the rejection or use of litanies, then this difference should have been indicated in some place of ancient liturgical books; however, this is not encountered. What the exclamation of the third litany should be is not indicated in any of the church books. Therefore we are forced to borrow it from Great Matins. At Great Matins in Slavonic books two exclamations are given which are used at the third litany, that is one -- for Sunday "For blessed is" and the second -- for Polyeleos feasts "For holy Art Thou". For the sake of distinguishing the Sunday day from the ordinary day, at Small Matins it is appropriate to use the second "For holy Art Thou", although it would not be bad to use also the first "For blessed is" (Οτι ευλογητοα), for in Greek books we find only this one exclamation, without distinction of day. Regarding Sessional Hymns, they are taken two or three after each stichology, depending on the edition, except the Theotokion. In Lviv editions of the *Octoechos* and in the Greek-Venetian one, only to the Theotokion is sung "Glory, Both now". In the Pochaiv and Moscow ones a separate refrain is also given to every second, or even to the third sessional hymn. This refrain, corresponding to the content of the sessional hymn: on Monday and Tuesday -- Penitential "O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger"; on Wednesday and Friday -- of the Cross, and on Thursday -- of the Apostles; to the third sessional hymn, that is the Martyron -- also the Martyron refrain: "Wondrous is God in His saints".

--- Match 51 [Terms: monasteries] ---
[311] Typikons of some Russian monasteries, given by the Tserkovne Oko, prescribe for the deacon at each Hour to sing three litanies, that is "In peace let us pray to the Lord" at the end of the tripalm, the Small Litany before the Prokeimenon and "Let us complete" after the singing of the Gospel. This is not enough for Greek length and with unnecessary additions the Greeks distort the form of the Hours, handed down to us from the beginnings; also ancient Lviv typikons add litanies.

--- Match 52 [Terms: ancient rule] ---
13.	Prokeimenon, Apostle, Alleluia and Gospel - only to the Forefathers (according to the ancient rule also to the saints, but now it is proper to take only for Saints with Polyeleos).

--- Match 53 [Terms: which today we usually do not take] ---
4.	On the Lytia: according to the Slavonic Typikon, first sticheron - of the temple (which today we usually do not take), also stichera to the saint. Glory: to the saint, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday Aposticha of Great Vespers, in the tone of the Doxastikon .

--- Match 54 [Terms: ancient rule] ---
[297] According to the ancient rule the service is taken also to the saint, if he has one, and the Apostle and Gospel -- first to the Saturday before the Nativity of Christ, sequential, according to the Slavic typikon are taken under the Prokeimenon (zaspiv) of the Saturday before the Nativity of Christ (*Menaion* 18 December and Moscow Typikon).

--- Match 55 [Terms: Monastery] ---
[201] According to the most general and most ancient typikons in the Afterfeast and on the Leavetaking the Trisagion should be taken, and not "As many as have been baptized into Christ" or "Before Thy Cross", for they are taken on the very day of the feast, with the exception of "As many as have been baptized into Christ", which is taken not only on the very day of the Resurrection, but also throughout the whole Bright Week, since throughout the whole Bright Week the divine service takes place as on the very day of the Resurrection. On other days, until the Leavetaking of the Resurrection, the Trisagion is taken. So have the general Greek and Slavonic typikons and the most ancient Typikon of the Monastery of St. Nicholas of Casole in the district of Otranto, kept in the Barberini library in Rome, written six hundred or seven hundred years ago. In it on fol. 121 and 124 is given the Typikon, which I gave. With this general and ancient typikon one Greek Typikon does not agree, written by the Reverend Father Bartholomew, a companion of the Reverend Father Nilus and co-founder of the Monastery of Grottaferrata near Rome, kept in that same monastery, which prescribes "As many as have been baptized into Christ" until the Leavetaking of the feast. E.g. December 26 gives: Και αντί του Τρισάγιου Οσοι εις Χριστον. Τούτο γαρ λεγομεν έως ου αποδιδοται η εορτή /And instead of the Trisagion As many as have been baptized into Christ. For this we say until the feast is taken leave of/. Does not agree also the new Constantinopolitan Typikon 1851, marked with the seal of the patriarch. See, e.g. on Monday of the Holy Spirit there, where Greek and Slavonic *Triodia* have the Trisagion, this Typikon has "As many as have been baptized into Christ". Regarding Troparia, Mark thus: Sunday, Glory: Troparion to the saint, if he has, Both now: Kontakion of the feast (Καιφ. ξε)/chapter 65/.

--- Match 56 [Terms: Monastery] ---
[183] 2 Ancient Greek Typikon of the Monastery of St. Nicholas των Κασουλων /of Casole/ (Ed. Note: a place in Puglia, southern Italy, where the Monastery of St. Nicholas was located) in the village of Otranto (Bibliotheca Barberina) prescribes "As many as have been baptized into Christ" also on the Transfiguration. And the Greek Typikon of Grottaferrata, that is of the monastery of the reverend father Nilus, near Rome, gives "As many as have been baptized into Christ" not only on the very day of the feast or in Bright Week, but also on all days of the Afterfeast until the Leavetaking of the feast; similarly also the new Constantinopolitan Typikon; however, the Greek Typikon, called of St. Sabbas, and following it also Slavonic typikons do not agree with that, for on Thomas Sunday and on Monday after the Descent of the Holy Spirit they explicitly prescribe the Trisagion. Does not agree also the Typikon, called of St. Nicholas.

--- Match 57 [Terms: ancient rule] ---
[285] According to the ancient rule the troparion of the saints is taken with Glory, also on the 1st and 6th hours; but with us today -- only the one of the day.

--- Match 58 [Terms: Monastic] ---
### Weekday Commemorations (Moscow Typikon)
- **Category:** *Liturgical Monastic & Parish Celebrations*
- **Source Reference:** *Footnote 334, L354*

--- Match 59 [Terms: ancient rule] ---
[334] The Moscow Typikon adds also the sequential (it is meant, if there is no dispensation); according to the ancient rule it would be necessary to add also to the saint, if he has something, however, for a saint without Polyeleos with us usually nothing is taken.

--- Match 60 [Terms: Monastic] ---
### Weekday Liturgy Commemorations (Greek vs. Slavonic)
- **Category:** *Liturgical Monastic & Parish Celebrations*
- **Source Reference:** *Footnote 91, L100*

--- Match 61 [Terms: monasteries, in monasteries] ---
[91] The Greek Typikon gives the whole service only to the saint, and Apostle and Gospel -- first of the day, and then of the saint; Προκειμενον του κατά την ημεραν Αγιου, Αλληλούια του Αγιου, Ευαγγελιον της ημέρας και του Αγιου. (Καιφ. σ) /Prokeimenon of the saint of the day, Alleluia of the saint, Gospel of the day and of the saint (chapter 6)/. So also in chapter 99, Mark. Although this rubric is given only in the Saturday service, however, it must be understood that also on other days of the week it should be so. Slavonic typikons prescribe the whole service to the day and to the saint, if he has one, and troparia -- also to the temple, if it is of the Lord or of the Theotokos. The Moscow Typikon also prescribes to the temple, if the temple is of a saint (although this does not agree with the rubric of that same Typikon, given on September 26 and January 30); this same Typikon ends the Kontakia with the Kontakion for the Dead "With the saints give rest" with the refrain "Glory" and the Kontakion of the temple, if it is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, if -- not, then with the usual "Protection" ("O Undisputed Intercessor") with the refrain "Both now" (chapter 52). This custom we find also among Greek-Italians and Greek-Melkites, but it does not exist among Constantinopolitan Greeks and Athonites, with the exception of some. But, since according to the rubric of the Greek Typikon, two Apostles and two Gospels were to be taken, and according to the Slavonic rubric, also the whole service of the day and of the saint, if he has one, and, since many saints (over one hundred eighty) have an Apostle and Gospel, as follows from the content of the Apostle-Gospel, therefore, in order to shorten the duration of the divine service, followers of the Greek Rite began already many years ago to reject certain rubrics; therefore also our Ruthenians take at the Liturgy only the service of the day, and reject the saint without Polyeleos; some -- conversely: take only the service to the saint, and reject the service of the day, as evidenced by the Pochaiv Service Book of 1765, which gives the Apostle and Gospel only on Sundays, and on weekdays -- only in Bright Week and in the week after the Descent of the Holy Spirit, similarly as Latin Missals, which give the Apostle and Gospel of the day only in these two mentioned weeks, for Great Lent and for certain other days. This custom we find also among Greek-Italians, observed also by some of those who are called Orthodox, whom the Moscow Typikon accuses, saying: "The Ecumenical Church does not take neither on the Beatitudes, nor at Liturgies the Apostle and Gospel to the saint on every day, only the sequential, that is the pillar throughout the whole year, in sequence, as also of selected saints who have an Apostle and Gospel. And if the Apostle and Gospel of the saint is read every day, then this is from one's own ignorance, and not according to the church typikon" (chapter 58, at the end). Exactly so also in the manuscript of the three-hundred-year-old Gospel that is among us. However, this rubric of the Ecumenical Church is not observed by the Constantinopolitan Hierarch, called Ecumenical, nor by the Churches subject to him, who (according to the testimony of hierarch Benjamin Νεαπολεος /of Neapolis/, who lived in Constantinople and on Mount Athos, who in our times accepted church union) have the custom, also in monasteries, to take the Apostle and Gospel only of the day, if the saint does not have one, if he has, then only to the saint, and in another they adhere to the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, printed in the patriarchal typography in 1851, marked with the patriarchal seal and confirmed by signatures of all patriarchs and hierarchs subject to them. In this Typikon there, where the rubric gives two Apostles and Gospels, one Apostle-Gospel is given, that is the Apostle -- of the first service, and the Gospel -- of the second service. Thus e.g. October 26, November 21, when they fall on a Sunday and on John the Theologian May 8, when it falls on Ascension.

With us, because the current Saint in the Menaion always has a troparion, then, if it were taken always, the troparia "O God of our Fathers" would be superfluous; so that they not be superfluous, they are taken daily, and to the Saints -- only to the Polyeleos ones. But in our current Horologia, troparia are given for every day of the month, and therefore at Matins we always sing "God is the Lord," and we limit the "Alleluia" service to Great Lent, as do the Greeks. May 8, when it falls on the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, September 20, November 23, December 4. If there be several Sessional Hymns, then "Glory" is sung to the last one, and "Both now" to its theotokion (Menaion, December 4; Lviv Synod, in the Order of Great Matins).

[^65]: Peremyshl Typikon, page 81. -- According to the ancient typikon there was to be a Sunday troparion at all hours, also "Glory" at the 1st and 6th -- Goes also to the first saint, at the 3rd and 9th -- to the second; Kontakia -- alternating: at the 1st and 6th -- to the first, at the 3rd and 9th -- to the second saint (compare John the Theologian on the Sunday of the Holy Fathers). When the Kontakion is only for one saint, then it alone is taken at all [hours]; and when for neither, nor for the feast, then the Hypakoe of the tone is taken; for general Greek editions do not have Sunday Kontakia. Regarding the Hypakoe, you have the rubric in Greek Horologions at the 1st Hour, it and at the other hours must be observed.
[^66]: Lviv Synod in heading 5. According to the Typikon given at the end of our Apostles, and in our previous ones, as also in some Greek service books in Goar, the daily antiphons "It is good" are given, also on Sundays, with the refrain only Sunday on the 3rd antiphon, as the Italo-Greeks also observe. General Greek service books give the Typika on Sunday, and Octoechoi also on weekdays; for in them are given daily stichera on the "Beatitudes", by which is understood the presence of the Typika. With such a difference of typikons let everyone preserve the custom of his Church, and among us the Typikon is -- of the Synod. In vain during the Synod some tried, saying, that to take daily antiphons, instead of the Typika, does not correspond to our rite; for daily antiphons are given not only by the aforementioned service books and apostles, but also by Greek Readers [Anagnostiki] and the Moscow Typikon in chapter 25, and St. Sophronius himself, Patriarch of Jerusalem, in the 7th century, in the explanation of the Divine Liturgy (Spicileg. Rom. tom. 4, nn. 12, 13, 14. Propag. 1840) -- mentions nothing about the Typika, but only about the antiphons "It is good". About the Typika we assume that they were left for the case when there is no Liturgy. For in the beginning numerous in monasticism were of the laity, who could not serve the Holy Liturgy; and therefore, instead of the Liturgy, they had to use the Order of Typika; therefore also the Synod limits them to days in which there is no Liturgy, so that in this way the memory of them was preserved only in monasteries. It is equally good to use antiphons, as also the Typika, when it is taken fully; but when from every psalm only one verse is taken and without stichera on the "Beatitudes", as they practice, then it is better to use antiphons. Finally, the full Typika is quite long and in present times was shortened.

[^69]: Troparia and other changeable parts of the Liturgy, according to the previous typikon, were not to be taken only of the Sunday, but also of the saint, if he has anything. (e. g. Μαρκ. Καιφ. ξη. and on verso. /Mark, chapters 68 and 79/; as also our Menaion September 7, if it falls on a Sunday). In previous typikons the saint sometimes did not have a troparion, and sometimes also a Kontakion; sometimes he had either only a troparion, or only a Kontakion, sometimes also a Prokimenon-Alleluia and Apostle and Gospel, and Communion Hymn. However, because later among us troparia and Kontakia were added to all saints, that in the sequence of the Menology, services to a saint without Polyeleos at the Liturgy are not taken; the Greeks, in the case of a saint who has an Apostle-Gospel on a Sunday began taking the Apostle only to the saint, and the Gospel -- only of the Sunday. New Constantinopolitan Typikon, 1851, e. g. September 3. Footnotes
[^70]: Search according to the content given at the beginning of the Psalter. The Psalter is divided into three parts, of which the first covers all weeks from the Sunday of Thomas the Apostle to Great Lent, the second -- Great Lent, the third -- Passion Week. In Bright Week the Psalter is not read.
[^71]: Since the Octoechos gives 6 stichera, and the Typikon does not determine which 3 of the 6 to take, we leave this to the judgment of the Reader. However, to say how it is better, let us first recall that the daily service of the Octoechos is double, that is: on Monday -- Penitential and to the Bodiless [Powers], on Tuesday -- Penitential and to the Forerunner, on Wednesday and Friday -- to the Cross and the Theotokos, on Thursday -- to the Apostles and Nicholas, on Saturday -- to all Saints and the Departed. Thus, in order not to discard either of the two services of the weekday, the Greek Octoechos (Παρακλητική /Parakletike/), printed in the "Propaganda" in 1738, gives from the three stichera of the first service only the two first stichera, and from the three stichera of the second service -- only one, the first. Among us, so that the printed stichera given in the Octoechos do not remain in vain, it would be possible to sing alternating all three stichera of the first service and all three stichera of the second service, that is, one year, odd -- three of the first service, and the second year, even -- three of the second service, or, even better -- one year two first stichera of the first service and one sticheron, the first, of the second service, and the second year -- one sticheron, the third, of the first service and two last of the second service. The Greek Typikon takes nothing from the Octoechos, but only three stichera from the Menaion on 6, repeating each (Καιφ. ε /chapter 5/). The Moscow Typikon takes three first stichera of the Theotokos from the Octoechos, which do not exist in our editions, but we find them in the Greek-Venetian edition; the Moscow Octoechos of 1782 has the same stichera as ours.
[^72]: In the Greek Menaion after the Doxastikon to the saint his Theotokion is also added. If there is no Doxastikon, then only certain most general Theotokia of the Octoechos are indicated by initial words; it is implied that they are known, although the number of Octoechos editions makes their search inconvenient. We cite here some: 1. Θεοτόκε, συ ει ο άμπελος "O Theotokos, Thou Art the vine" (Venetian Octoechos, Tone 6, on the Aposticha); θεον σαρκοΰεντα εκ σου "God incarnate from Thee" (Tone 6, on Saturday, Praises, Moscow edition); Δέσποινα προσδεξαι "O Sovereign Lady, receive" (Tone 8, on Saturday at Matins; Την πασαν ελπίδα "All my hope" (Tone 2, on Thursday, on the Aposticha of Matins). Sometimes the Theotokion is not indicated even by initial words, but is only named generally: θεοτοκιον ομοιον /Theotokion similar/, or only: θεοτοκιον /Theotokion/ (Menaion September 3, August 2); in this case one must select a Theotokion from the Octoechos. Four aforementioned Theotokia are given also on "Lord, I have cried", and on the Aposticha (Menaion November 1 and 3, December 5, July 1). In the last edition of the Venetian Menaion Theotokia are given almost always fully. But among us, at the end of every volume of the Menaion are given all Theotokia that are taken on "Lord, I have cried" and on the Aposticha, and not only for every tone, but also for every day of the week, in order not to repeat the same Theotokia many times. In the Pochaiv Anthologion Theotokia are given only according to the tone, and not also according to the day; and in general services sometimes only the first words are cited, and they themselves are not given.

[^77]: When two kathismata should be read, and when three, we noted in the note to the Order of Daily Matins on page 41. Regarding small litanies which are read between kathismata and sessional hymns, the Lviv Synod, our Horologions and our Sluzhebnyky (at the end) give them also at Small Matins, and they are used also by the Greek-Melkites (according to the testimony of their patriarch bl. m. Clement Bahous). Conversely, in the Russian Typikon, approved by their Governing Synod (Moscow edition of 1859), in the Order of Daily Matins it is written that the Priest says them only at the Vigil and when the memory of saints having a Polyeleos and Doxology is celebrated; similarly also in the new Wallachian-Bucharest Typikon, in which in the Order of Daily Matins is given: "We take sessional hymns from the Octoechos without litanies" (untranslatable); and adds: "In monasteries, usually, between kathismata litanies are taken only on Sunday and when more special saints occur". From the Greek Typikon no conclusion can be made, for in it small litanies are not mentioned neither at Small Matins, nor at Great, nor before the Canon, nor after the Canon. It is assumed in the Typikon that this is known to everyone. From the rubrics of the Greek Triodion, given at Matins on Meatfare Saturday, one can conclude that these litanies should be said also at Small Matins. For it gives: η συνηιϊης στιχολογια. Εν δε ταις συναπταις πασαις της στιχολογιας και του Κανόνος, μνημονεύονται πάντες οι προαπελΰοντες Πατέρες και αδελφοί ημων /Ordinary Stichology. In all small litanies of the Stichology and of the Canon, all our fathers and brethren who have passed away before us are commemorated/. Let everyone observe the rubric and custom of his Church. Finally, in our opinion, later typikons reject litanies here only for the sake of greater shortening of the divine service; if the single difference between Small and Great Matins were the rejection or use of litanies, then this difference should have been indicated in some place of ancient liturgical books; however, this is not encountered. What the exclamation of the third litany should be is not indicated in any of the church books. Therefore we are forced to borrow it from Great Matins. At Great Matins in Slavonic books two exclamations are given which are used at the third litany, that is one -- for Sunday "For blessed is" and the second -- for Polyeleos feasts "For holy Art Thou". For the sake of distinguishing the Sunday day from the ordinary day, at Small Matins it is appropriate to use the second "For holy Art Thou", although it would not be bad to use also the first "For blessed is" (Οτι ευλογητοα), for in Greek books we find only this one exclamation, without distinction of day. Regarding Sessional Hymns, they are taken two or three after each stichology, depending on the edition, except the Theotokion. In Lviv editions of the Octoechos and in the Greek-Venetian one, only to the Theotokion is sung "Glory, Both now". In the Pochaiv and Moscow ones a separate refrain is also given to every second, or even to the third sessional hymn. This refrain, corresponding to the content of the sessional hymn: on Monday and Tuesday -- Penitential "O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger"; on Wednesday and Friday -- of the Cross, and on Thursday -- of the Apostles; to the third sessional hymn, that is the Martyron -- also the Martyron refrain: "Wondrous is God in His saints".

[^91]: The Greek Typikon gives the whole service only to the saint, and Apostle and Gospel -- first of the day, and then of the saint; Προκειμενον του κατά την ημεραν Αγιου, Αλληλούια του Αγιου, Ευαγγελιον της ημέρας και του Αγιου. (Καιφ. σ) /Prokimenon of the saint of the day, Alleluia of the saint, Gospel of the day and of the saint (chapter 6)/. So also in chapter 99, Mark. Although this rubric is given only in the Saturday service, however, it must be understood that also on other days of the week it should be so. Slavonic typikons prescribe the whole service to the day and to the saint, if he has one, and troparia -- also to the temple, if it is of the Lord or of the Theotokos. The Moscow Typikon also prescribes to the temple, if the temple is of a saint (although this does not agree with the rubric of that same Typikon, given on September 26 and January 30); this same Typikon ends the Kontakia with the Kontakion for the Dead "With the saints give rest" with the refrain "Glory" and the Kontakion of the temple, if it is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, if -- not, then with the usual "Protection" ("O Undisputed Intercessor") with the refrain "Both now" (chapter 52). This custom we find also among Greek-Italians and Greek-Melkites, but it does not exist among Constantinopolitan Greeks and Athonites, with the exception of some. But, since according to the rubric of the Greek Typikon, two Apostles and two Gospels were to be taken, and according to the Slavonic rubric, also the whole service of the day and of the saint, if he has one, and, since many saints (over one hundred eighty) have an Apostle and Gospel, as follows from the content of the Apostle-Gospel, therefore, in order to shorten the duration of the divine service, followers of the Greek Rite began already many years ago to reject certain rubrics; therefore also our Ruthenians take at the Liturgy only the service of the day, and reject the saint without Polyeleos; some -- conversely: take only the service to the saint, and reject the service of the day, as evidenced by the Pochaiv Sluzhebnik of 1765, which gives the Apostle and Gospel only on Sundays, and on weekdays -- only in Bright Week and in the week after the Descent of the Holy Spirit, similarly as Latin Missals, which give the Apostle and Gospel of the day only in these two mentioned weeks, for Great Lent and for certain other days. This custom we find also among Greek-Italians, observed also by some of those who are called Orthodox, whom the Moscow Typikon accuses, saying: "The Ecumenical Church does not take neither on the Beatitudes, nor at Liturgies the Apostle and Gospel to the saint on every day, only the sequential, that is the pillar throughout the whole year, in sequence, as also of selected saints who have an Apostle and Gospel. And if the Apostle and Gospel of the saint is read every day, then this is from one's own ignorance, and not according to the church typikon" (chapter 58, at the end). Exactly so also in the manuscript of the three-hundred-year-old Gospel that is among us. However, this rubric of the Ecumenical Church is not observed by the Constantinopolitan Hierarch, called Ecumenical, nor by the Churches subject to him, who (according to the testimony of hierarch Benjamin Νεαπολεος /of Neapolis/, who lived in Constantinople and on Mount Athos, who in our times accepted church union) have the custom, also in monasteries, to take the Apostle and Gospel only of the day, if the saint does not have one, if he has, then only to the saint, and in another they adhere to the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, printed in the patriarchal typography in 1851, marked with the patriarchal seal and confirmed by signatures of all patriarchs and hierarchs subject to them. In this Typikon there, where the rubric gives two Apostles and Gospels, one Apostle-Gospel is given, that is the Apostle -- of the first service, and the Gospel -- of the second service. Thus e. g. October 26, November 21, when they fall on a Sunday and on John the Theologian May 8, when it falls on Ascension.

When someone reads in the weeks the Apostle and Gospel only to the saint, or only for a certain intention, does not need to take it also to the feast, but only then, when he reads the Apostle-Gospel of the day.
[^197]: About the commemoration of the feast at the Dismissal in the Afterfeast we find nothing in Greek typikons. From the Typikon of Slavonic Triodia and of the Moscow Typikon one could conclude that the commemoration of the feast should be taken only on the very solemn day of the feast and on its Apodosis. For it prescribes to take the Paschal Dismissal throughout the whole Bright Week, every day of which has the form of the divine service of the very day of the Resurrection, and afterwards prescribes to take it at the Liturgy of the Apodosis of the Resurrection. However, today we observe the general custom to make the festal commemoration also on other days of the Afterfeast, which has also the rubric of the Sluzhebnik, printed in Stratyn under the Lviv Bishop Gedeon Balaban in 1604, 11 years before the Tserkovne Oko, which gives the following: "Know, that festal dismissals are taken from the day of the feast until the Apodosis of the feast of every day" (page 517). Similarly one could take in Theotokion Afterfeasts, but the words "today" and "solemnly" must be discarded. However, in Afterfeasts of the Lord it is better not to take the commemoration of the feast on Sunday, for it does not combine well with the Sunday commemoration, which must occupy the first place. It would be, e. g. on the Afterfeast of the Nativity of Christ: "Who rose from the dead and Who was born in a cavern in Bethlehem of Judea and let Himself be laid in a manger for our salvation, Christ, our true God". And on weekdays, except Sunday, the commemoration of the feast can be taken without doubt, for then the service of the feast replaces the service of the Octoechos and therefore, instead of the commemoration of the service of the day, that is of the Octoechos, it is good to take the commemoration of the service of the feast. Some on Sunday discard the Sunday commemoration and take only of the feast, but they act contrary to our Sluzhebnyky, which on the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, in the middle of the feast of the Ascension before Pentecost, give the Sunday commemoration.
